Arsenal have suffered a significant setback in their pursuit of RB Leipzig winger Antonio Nusa, with Fabrizio Romano suggesting a January transfer is unlikely unless the Gunners make an extraordinary offer.

The Norwegian international has emerged as one of Europe’s most exciting attacking prospects, attracting considerable attention following his impressive performances in Germany. Mikel Arteta is keen to strengthen his attacking options, and Nusa has reportedly become a priority target ahead of the winter transfer window.

Arsenal have been particularly short of quality options on the left flank following the departures of Gabriel Martinelli and Leandro Trossard. Although Christos Tzolis arrived during the summer, the Gunners could benefit from another versatile attacker capable of making an immediate impact.

Nusa has enjoyed an impressive campaign with Leipzig, registering five goals and seven assists across competitions. His pace, technical ability and creativity could make him an excellent addition to Arteta’s attacking setup.

However, securing his signature could prove considerably more complicated than Arsenal would have hoped.

On his YouTube channel, Romano said Leipzig have set a starting valuation of approximately €70-75 million for the 21-year-old.

The Bundesliga outfit considers Nusa one of the most exciting wingers available and has little intention of negotiating a reduced transfer fee.

More importantly, Leipzig would prefer to retain the Norwegian until the summer before considering suitable offers.

Romano believes a January departure remains unlikely, explaining that only an extraordinary financial proposal could persuade the German club to reconsider their position.

“My understanding is that RB Leipzig start at around €70-75 million for Antonio Nusa, so he’s quite expensive.

“Leipzig believe he’s one of the best wingers on the market, and they will not offer any discount for Nusa.

“It’s also important to say that Leipzig’s expectation is to continue with the player until the summer transfer window and then consider a potential exit if a good proposal arrives.

“They are not pushing to sell Nusa in January. I think the possibility of him leaving in January is not particularly concrete. It’s not easy. Either there is a completely crazy bid or, for Nusa, it’s going to be the summer.”

The Gunners have the financial resources to pursue an ambitious deal, but paying a substantial premium midway through the season might not be straightforward.

Nusa could add another dimension in the final third, particularly with his ability to beat defenders and create chances.

Nevertheless, Arteta’s side may have to remain patient and revisit their interest next summer if Leipzig refuse to change their stance.

“Train Daddy” Morgan Spector Is Suddenly a Sex Symbol

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

At 46, ‘The Gilded Age’ star is becoming a leading man as the new Robert Langdon — and an object of internet obsession.

Twenty years after Morgan Spector arrived in New York hoping to make a living as an actor, he has finally reached the point where he’s No. 1 on the call sheet. All it took was becoming a sex symbol at 46.

After years spent bouncing between theater and prestige television, Spector’s turn as railroad baron George Russell on HBO’s The Gilded Age has made him an object of internet lust — “Train Daddy,” to a particularly enthusiastic segment of the show’s fan base — and given him the sort of platform he’s still figuring out what to do with. And now comes an even bigger leap: Spector is taking over as Robert Langdon, the globe-trotting Harvard symbologist Tom Hanks played in three blockbuster movies, in Netflix’s upcoming adaptation of Dan Brown’s The Secret of Secrets. It’s easily the biggest role of Spector’s career. Related Stories TV 'The Gilded Age' Is Serving Up Ambition and Shame in Season 4 Return Movies 'Moonfish' Review: Morgan Spector and Lucy Liu Voice a Spare but Evocative Animated Sci-Fi Suffused With Loneliness

Although Spector is quick to qualify the achievement. “But my wife is No. 2,” he says, referring to spouse and Secrets co-star Rebecca Hall. “So I don’t get to eat all the green M&Ms on set, if you know what I mean.”

Spector is currently experiencing one of the more interesting midcareer moments in Hollywood. Until relatively recently, he seemed comfortably settled into being one of those reliably excellent performers whose face audiences might recognize even if his name didn’t immediately come to mind.

His growing celebrity has presented another complication. “My era of becoming somebody with a platform has also been a time of dramatic political change, and I’m an outspoken person,” Spector says. “Sometimes I’d like to get off social media, but then I’m abandoning a space that I can use to say things that matter. But then, I have to do all the things that make people want to come to the platform, which means taking pretty pictures of yourself. There’s only so much reposting of ‘Democracy Now’ that your average Instagram viewer wants  to see.”

In the years before The Gilded Age, Spector was doing what actors have traditionally done when nobody was asking them for selfies: looking for work. After graduating from Reed College and the American Conservatory Theater in San Francisco, he moved to New York, where in 2010 a two-line walk-on in the Broadway production of A View From the Bridge turned into a starring role opposite Liev Schreiber and Scarlett Johansson.

“I was living off Nostrand Avenue in Brooklyn at the time, and I would commute in to work every day in a state of euphoria,” he says. “It just felt like New York City was wrapping its arms around me and giving me all I’ve ever dreamed of. But then I had to calibrate and think, ‘OK, well, what else is possible now?’ “

For a while, the answer was more work rather than stardom. Roles on Homeland and miniseries The Plot Against America followed, and when Spector auditioned to play George Russell on The Gilded Age, he saw the Julian Fellowes drama as a chance to add another solid credit — and, with any luck, a steady job — to his résumé.

“They originally talked about five seasons as being a way to complete the arc,” Spector says. “But I saw that [HBO head of drama] Francesca Orsi say she’d love it to run for 10 years, so who knows?”

What he didn’t foresee was the way audiences would respond to George. “Somebody wrote a piece about how George and Bertha’s [Carrie Coon] marriage was the sexiest thing on television, and I remember thinking, ‘What?!’ ” he says with a laugh. “The show is relatively chaste, especially for HBO, and we weren’t really lingering in eroticism. But it was the first sign that, OK, people are connecting to this in a way that wasn’t obvious to us as we were making it.”

Then came Robert Langdon. When Spector read for The Secret of Secrets with showrunner Carlton Cuse, he was reminded of his Gilded Age audition — another opportunity he wasn’t entirely sure made sense for him.

“I didn’t think I was right for the part, and I guess as I’m getting older, I’m realizing that I don’t know what’s going to work for me or what I’m going to be good at,” Spector adds with a laugh.

He is now filming The Secret of Secrets in Prague. The acting part, at least, he knows how to handle. The rest of his newfound celebrity is proving more complicated.

“They don’t teach you about this part in school,” he says. “It’s like, how many thirst traps are enough and how many are too many? We’re making our way through the wilderness.”

Riccardo Calafiori faces Arsenal axe after Italy 'nightmare' - verdict

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Correspondent AUTHORITY Sports journalist with experience at GamingINTEL and GRV Media; University of Wolverhampton graduate. FOCUS Data content, opinion, and general news coverage across the Breaking Media network. THE INSIGHT Scott utilises a network of club and industry contacts to deliver verified, broad-spectrum reporting. He provides the data and opinion foundations behind the headlines to ensure fans get the full story as it happens.

Riccardo Calafiori had an evening to forget for Italy, and it may harm his chances back at Arsenal.

The defender was part of the Italian side that lost 4-1 to Belgium, leaving them facing relegation play-offs for League A.

The 24-year-old has been a mainstay in Arsenal's squad this season, starting nine of the 10 Premier League games so far.

Mikel Arteta clearly trusts the talent the Italian has to offer, but ahead of facing Newcastle this weekend, he may have something to think about.

While he was away on international duty, Calafiori came up against a player that offers a similar threat to what Arsenal are set to face.

And he did not cover himself in glory for his nation.

Arteta will be well aware of the threat Elanga poses for Arsenal this weekend, with his selection of fullbacks all the more important against Newcastle.

Calafiori's performance against Belgium will do little to reassure the Arsenal boss that he is the right man to handle that challenge.

According to Italian outlet Eurosport, Calafiori's evening against Belgium was a "nightmare."

The fullback was unable to deal with the speed and pace of winger Diego Moreria, which may prompt some fears back at the Emirates.

When domestic football returns, the left-back has a possible match-up against Anthony Elanga looming, with Newcastle awaiting.

Like Moreira, Elanga is a pacy winger who often looks to beat his man down the wing.

On Sunday evening, Calafiori did not deal with that well, with Sofascore showing he won just one of his three attempted tackles during the game - a 33 per cent success rate.

He also only won 50 per cent of his duels, showcasing that it was a mixed evening for him against the AC Milan star.

Moreira was also able to get himself on the scoresheet up against the Arsenal man.

With Calafiori's struggles evident, that could give Arteta something to ponder, as Elanga is likely to provide the same headache he has just faced and failed to deliver on.

Arsenal have plenty of options at left-back, with Piero Hincapie one of the choices that could be turned to.

Additionally, Jurrien Timber is also capable of playing on either side of defence, with Myles Lewis-Skelly also among Arteta's choices.

Of course, the Spaniard could view Calafiori's poor display as a one-off bad night, but his "nightmare" against Belgium should give his club manager something to think about.

Especially with Newcastle's direct and vertical football, which will look to exploit pace down the wings.

HOA Trade Group Presses for More Protections After DC Shooter Targeted Building Manager

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The trade group representing homeowners associations is calling for stronger protections after a shooting in Washington, DC, left a community manager dead.

The Community Association Institute said that it would renew efforts support laws that address harassment, threats, and intimidation against community managers before they escalate into violence.

The group had pressed for such legislation in Nevada in 2025 and plans to do so at the federal level in 2027. New Jersey and Minnesota are also reportedly considering some legislation to address harassment.

Thursday's shooting in DC's Logan Circle neighborhood claimed the life of James “Jimmy” Stuart, manager of the Metropole and injured five others. The suspected shooter, Dr. Matt Wade Lambert, a resident of the complex, reportedly was in a dispute with the community over renovations he said damaged his penthouse unit.

“We mourn Jimmy's loss and extend our deepest condolences to his family, friends, colleagues, and the entire Metropole community,” the CAI statement reads. “Our thoughts are also with the other victims, residents, and building staff whose lives have been forever changed by this tragedy.”

Lambert was in the middle of a protracted legal dispute with his building's HOA at the time that the shooting happened.

Social median posts reputedly made by him criticized the community for months after he alleged repairs caused water damage and mold. Police say that, after Lambert killed Stuart and injured four others, he subsequently took his own life during a police standoff.

According to legal filings, Lambert had incurred damages topping $100,000 as a result of the HOA's "failure to take corrective action to remedy the moisture accumulation."

“No one should have to fear for their safety while doing their job or serving their community. Community association managers, building staff, and volunteer board members dedicate themselves to maintaining the places millions of Americans call home. They deserve to carry out those responsibilities without threats, harassment, intimidation, or violence,” the statement concludes.

Tristan Navera is a senior reporter on housing policy, covering trends and solutions in the housing market from Washington, DC. He was previously a senior reporter at Bloomberg Law, and before that covered real estate for the Washington Business Journal. Earlier in his career, he spent a decade reporting on business and real estate in Dayton and Columbus, OH. A Cincinnati native, he holds a journalism degree from Ohio University.

Liga: Real Madrid crushes Valencia and temporarily returns to Barça

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Evening in peaceful waters for Real Madrid. Against Valencia this Saturday, the Merengues scored an eighth consecutive success in all competitions (0-3). It wasn’t the most complicated of the series, but it was certainly the most emotionally significant with an evening dedicated to Jesús De Miguel, who died at the start of the week during a Spanish second division match. The former Tenerife player was honored at kick-off, before receiving a moving tribute from Mestalla in the 9th minute of play, in reference to his jersey number.

In this very particular context, the Madrilenians will have needed a little less than thirty minutes to play their football and extinguish any suspense. The only real warnings in favor of the locals? A loss of ball from Tchouameni forcing Courtois to work in front of Mayol (19th), then this goal refused to Rodriguez for offside a few seconds later (20th). As against AEK Athens in midweek, Federico Valverde played a central role in his team’s success. Preferred over Denzel Dumfries and Trent Alexander-Arnold at right-back, it was he who opened the scoring with a beautiful half-volley following a free kick from Kylian Mbappé deflected by the wall (28th, 0-1).

Left to rest for the trip to Greece on Wednesday in the Champions League (2-3), Arda Güler made the break after an outburst from Bellingham (36th, 0-2). The reward for a top match, which logically earned him the title of man of the match. A little less prominent than in his last outings, Yan Diomandé missed a 0-3 ball before the break (42nd). And yet, Madrid’s domination seemed to elude Pablo Maffeo, who let his frustration explode at half-time by pointing the finger at the refereeing decisions. A barely daring exit from the right side, more busy distributing blows during the first act.

The second one-sided period proved Madrilenians right: Güler (55th), Mbappé (56th, 57th), then Mbappé again on an attempted return (65th), had opportunities to make the decision. But through insistence, the captain of the Blues finally found the solution in a Güler caviar. He eliminated Dimitrievski and scored his 13th goal of the season (75th, 0-3). Gaya was sent off for a foul on the Madrid number 10 (79th), who was then entitled to a friendly chant from the local supporters: “Mbappé beach ball”, then to whistles as he left. Mbappé will still leave Mestalla with the feeling of accomplishment this evening: he is two units behind Raphinha in the La Liga scorers’ ranking, and Real Madrid is temporarily back at the level of FC Barcelona.

‘The Odyssey’ Cinematographer Hoyte Van Hoytema Breaks Down Filming Climactic Thunderstorm Scene in Imax

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Christopher Nolan shot “The Odyssey” to be experienced on the biggest screen possible so audiences could feel the full impact and be fully immersed in that experience — so everything was shot practically.

One of the most challenging sequences to pull off was the film’s final storm scene as Matt Damon’s Odysseus faces the wrath of Poseidon and the full force of Zeus. It was to be climactic and intense, and Odysseus loses all his men as a result of the final insult to the gods. Related Stories Netflix Names Mattel Executive Roberto Stanichi as VP of Consumer Products and Experiences 'Russell Brand: Open Secret' Director Says Finding Contributors Wasn't Easy: 'There's Been a Landscape of Fear'

“We had been talking about going out there to sea, sit on real boats and shoot on Imax,” cinematographer Hoyte Van Hoytema explains.

A large amount of the film was shot on the open ocean, where Nolan, his cast and crew spent four months capturing how hard the journey would have been as they endured crashing waves. And while the climactic moment ended up being shot in Los Angeles, Nolan didn’t spare any expense in pulling off the sequence, relying instead on old-fashioned filmmaking.Speaking with Variety for Inside the Frame, Van Hoytema explains the scene provided a challenge: “How to replicate something and make it feel visceral and real? How can we do that and avoid a huge amount of CG?”Two decommissioned jet engines were used as wind machines. Van Hoytema’s chief lighting technician and gaffer Adam Chambers built light boxes that would emulate thunder and lightning. The boat was then put on a gimbal, all while being shot on an Imax camera. “It was like shooting a real storm, just under safety supervision,” Van Hoytema says.He adds, “We did a lot of different setups in that whole scene. It is designed to feel extremely chaotic; it’s designed to sort of not understand really what’s going on.”The lighting design was something he also had to think about. “It was interesting because I was thinking, if you’re out at sea at night 2,000 years ago, without electricity and you end up in a thunderstorm, what would that feel like? I was thinking, if you can see everything around you, that’s going to look fake.”

A CGI storm didn’t feel right because the entire crew had been meticulous about keeping everything as grounded and as experiential as possible. Van Hoytema says the team built overhead lighting rigs that were approximately 60×30 feet, hanging on cranes above the ship. “It meant that you would see flashes that progressively move from behind them to the front of them, as it opens up the sky. “What you see is short bursts and there’s just darkness or a little bit of a glow. It’s like trying to see this relentless endless sea, this weather, this saltwater and they’re just so close to death. It’s extremely claustrophobic at the same time.”

When it came to framing Odysseus, Van Hoytema was wired in to keep him in place on the gimbal. His Imax camera on his shoulder was also wired in.

“I was not trying to be in any way precious about framing whatsoever. I was just trying through with a jet engine blowing, gallons of water in my face. I was literally just trying to identify if it was even Matt Damon in the shot. It was extremely visceral, and it’s almost hard to grasp. It was not a situation in which you could just start a take or not. Every take was an event.”

Van Hoytema says the camera starts to roll and the jet engines start. “They rise, and at some point, they sound like two fighter jets are next to your ear. And it gets really wild because they start moving the gimbal as well, and you’re holding on for your life. The camera gets turned; it’s the original Imax camera, not the blimp and the Imax camera is very loud. However, in that situation, you don’t even know if it’s turned on. You do a take, and you have a crazy powerful adrenaline rush.”

In contrast, Van Hoytema recalled a moment when the crew was out to sea, in open waters and a pod of bottlenose dolphins appeared. It was completely unplanned.

Van Hoytema recalls, “It’s interesting because it was also pretty violent that day. It was very harsh weather and I remember a lot of people on the crew got seasick. As we started filming, dolphins started to jump out of the water. What is interesting is that it symbolizes that no matter what the weather does, we take the weather as it comes and accept it as the reality you work in that day.”

Instagram creators can now tag 30 Amazon products in a single Reel

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

US creators can tag up to 30 Amazon products in a Reel and earn a commission on each sale.

Instagram now lets US creators tag Amazon products in Reels and posts.

Instagram now lets US creators in Amazon’s influencer program add Amazon affiliate links directly to Reels and feed posts, with up to 30 products per Reel. Meta takes no cut. The feature was first promised in March and arrives just before Black Friday.

Instagram creators in the US can now add Amazon affiliate links straight to their Reels and feed posts, Meta announced on Monday. Viewers who tap a product go to the Amazon app, and the creator earns a commission on any sale, TechCrunch reported.

Creators can tag up to 30 Amazon products in a Reel and 20 in a feed post. They link their Amazon account in Instagram’s settings, and the app shows the expected commission for each item before they post. Meta says it takes no cut and charges no fees.

For now, the feature is only for US creators aged 18 or older who have a professional account and are in Amazon’s influencer program, according to Engadget. Meta has not said if it will reach other countries.

Meta says links on posts get about twice as many clicks as links in a creator’s bio. That is bad news for the “link in bio” services many influencers use, because Instagram has long limited where links can go. Paid Meta One plans now also let creators put links in their posts.

Meta may not take a cut, but the links tell it what its users want to buy, Engadget noted.

The feature has been a while coming. Meta first showed 30-product tagging for Reels in March and said Amazon links would reach Instagram “this spring”. Facebook creators in the US have been able to earn from Amazon, eBay and Temu links since then.

Instagram is fighting TikTok and YouTube for creators. Tools built for TikTok Shop sellers are moving to Instagram too. Instagram is also reportedly offering YouTubers up to $500K to post long videos to the app.

For Amazon, the links pull more shoppers into its own app, where it wants its Alexa for Shopping agent to become the main way people buy.

Gravemark is a roguelike dungeon crawler you can play in portrait mode, out now on iOS

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ever since 198X's Rogue, the idea of venturing into a dungeon, grabbing loot and attempting to exit without being killed has thrilled hardcore players. No surprise then, that Gravemark, a portrait-mode roguelike, has really caught our eye. And now it's not just Android players able to enjoy it, as Gravemark hits iOS!

At its core, Gravemark is your stereotypical roguelike. Venture into the deep dungeon, automatically battle enemies while selecting skills and potions, then decide what to put into your limited inventory space before choosing whether to plunge deeper or get out. The twists are the aforementioned auto-battles, limited inventory, and the fact you don't actually know what the loot is until you take it back to be identified.

About the only unfortunate thing about Gravemark is that, you guessed it, there are hints of AI about it, especially with some of the key art. It's a shame because the core gameplay seems rock solid. And while I'm sure for some it won't detract, for me it does colour my perception of the game a bit.

If you're willing to give Gravemark a go regardless, you can find it out now on iOS and available already via Android, free to play.

Or, if you'd prefer a slightly more traditional (and forgiving) experience, why not check out our list of the best RPGs on Android, for adventures through worlds of fantasy and sci-fi?

Shondaland Taps Amy Joy Rivkin and Karyn Smith-Forge for Key Executive Roles

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Rivkin will be vp production at the company, and Smith-Forge will serve as vp scripted series.

Shondaland is adding to its executive ranks.

The production company founded by Shonda Rhimes and Betsy Beers has named Amy Joy Rivkin as vp production and Karyn Smith-Forge as vp scripted series. Rivkin comes to Shondaland from Media Res, and Smith-Forge from Don Cheadle’s production company This Radicle Act.

“Amy Joy’s impressive track record steering complex, premium productions and Karyn’s vast experience and sharp instincts across scripted genres make them powerful additions to Shondaland,” said Alison Eakle, Shondaland’s chief content officer. “We’re thrilled we get to shape our next chapter of storytelling alongside them.” Related Stories TV Shonda Rhimes Signs New Overall Deal at Netflix TV Kim Raver on Leaving 'Grey's Anatomy': "Who Gets to Play a Character for This Long?"

Rivkin will oversee physical production on Shondaland’s shows, which include Netflix’s Bridgerton, ABC’s Grey’s Anatomy and its upcoming spinoff and a number of projects in development. She spent the past four years at The Morning Show and Pachinko producer Media Res. Prior to that, she was at Netflix, handling production management for the streamer’s original drama series, and Legendary Entertainment.

As vp scripted series, Smith-Forge will lead development of new series from Shondaland at Netflix, where the company has a rich overall deal. Smith-Forge was most recently president of This Radicle Act, working with Cheadle on TV and film projects at a number of outlets, including Netflix, Showtime, AMC, FX and HBO Max.

She was previously senior vp production at Berlanti Productions, where she managed a slate of some 20 series, including The CW’s Arrow-verse.

I Took a Mystery “Decision Detox” Trip With Zero Planning. Here’s What Happened.

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

From the treehouse-like deck outside my hotel room, I took in the lush cluster of live oak and eucalyptus trees all around me, the whisper of wind and chirp of unknown birds, and the small sliver of Pacific Ocean peeking through the foliage-lined horizon. I was completely at peace. The best part: I hadn’t made a single decision for the entire weekend. In fact, until just a couple days prior—the day of my trip departure—I had absolutely no idea where I’d be on this gorgeous Sunday morning.

The morning of my trip, I tore open a sealed envelope that held all the information about the hotel where I’d be staying (Chaminade Resort and Spa), my itinerary (forest therapy, spa treatments, dining at The View and Lago di Como), and even the destination itself (Santa Cruz, California). This was all part of the “mystery travel” experience I eagerly agreed to, facilitated by Benchmark Resorts & Hotels and Pack Up + Go, an agency that plans completely curated surprise vacations. Here was the deal: I filled out a survey about my preferences (my priorities while traveling, the types of hotels I liked) along with budget, then they planned the entire trip, and I wouldn’t find out details (beyond some notes about weather and packing suggestions) until I opened my envelope the morning of my departure.

As an avid traveler, eldest daughter, and strategic planner (particularly in the context of trips), I thought this format might stress me out—but I found there was a sense of freedom and lightness in not pouring into pre-trip research or curating my own itinerary. All I had to do was show up. This brand of mental surrender actually speaks to a rising travel trend: “decision detox.”

Modern life can often feel like an endless series of choices. Between social media, the internet, and now AI, “we are inundated with information and access to it,” says Morgan Cutlip, PhD, psychology educator, author, and researcher. “This sends us regularly searching for the ‘right’ way of doing things rather than trusting our instincts or talking to a friend and doing what they do.”

And that absolutely applies to trip planning. “When there are so many options for something like a vacation, the more information you consume and the more reviews you read, the more uncertain you can become about your decision,” she says. “Searching out accommodations, looking up flight options, thinking through food—it becomes a ton of micro decisions packed into this one major thing that feels like it’s supposed to be extra magical, extra fun, and low stress, so the stakes feel high.”

In fact, while vacations are meant to be a time to unwind and enjoy life, a 2025 survey of 1,500 Americans from YouGov found 70 percent of U.S. vacationers find their booking experience to be stress-inducing. That’s where “decision detox” trips come in, where you completely relinquish control of trip planning. The intention here is for travelers to take a break from the mental overload of everyday life and never-ending choices, in order to take a true vacation.

This travel trend is on the rise. A research report published by Lemongrass, a certified B-Corp marketing agency, found a clear shift in traveler preferences for curated, all-inclusive itineraries that remove planning fatigue; a desire to visit quieter, less-crowded destinations; and a pull toward peaceful relaxation over highly stimulating experiences.

And while traditional travel agents have long been planning accommodations and transportation, decision detox trips take that concept one step further with an emphasis on fully planned retreats and completely handled logistics. In my case, this meant a complete surprise trip and fully embracing the thrill of adventure that comes with not knowing your destination until the moment you depart.

“For some people, this might sound like handcuffs on a holiday, but for [people] burdened with making seemingly every decision in their families’ lives all the time, it’s heaven in the form of a pre-booked massage and meals that magically appear when desired,” Jools Sampson, founder and owner of Reclaim Yourself Retreats, wrote in the Lemongrass report.

Women are likely disproportionately affected by this kind of decision fatigue, as they tend to take on the lion’s share of trip planning. In fact, data from research organization Skift found that 82 percent of all travel decisions are made by women. This can be particularly draining for moms, who are constantly managing the logistics of life, says Cutlip, whose work often focuses on mental load discrepancies in relationships. “And if she’s the one planning it, she’s almost certainly coordinating with her partner too. Not being able to fully make decisions on her own, having to negotiate and talk each one through, adds yet another layer of work.”

But no matter what role someone takes in a family structure, or what the underlying reason is for mental fatigue, leaning into decision detox travel can come with a whole slew of mental health benefits.

“You eliminate all of the fatigue, overwhelm, and unnecessary stress that can come with planning a trip,” says Cutlip. “If you’re somebody who has the capacity to let go and you’re comfortable putting someone else in the driver’s seat, it frees you to focus on everything else a vacation requires: preparing to leave your home, getting work off, taking care of the pets, cleaning out the fridge, and then showing up ready to relax.”

Benchmark decided to partner with Pack Up + Go after reading YouGov’s survey about traveler planning fatigue. “It’s a different way to approach a vacation,” says Evan Crawford, vice president of marketing for Benchmark Resorts & Hotels.

It also leads to travelers visiting locations they may not have otherwise thought to book—such as Woodinville, Washington, or the Columbia River Gorge, says Crawford. “Maybe you’ve never even heard of the place before, but now you can go there and get exposed to it and have this extraordinary experience.”

And while a complete surprise vacation is one approach, it’s not the only way to try out a decision detox trip.

A reprieve from decision-making is at the heart of Duvine Cycling, which curates bike tour vacations that take guests everywhere from the vineyards of Burgundy to the Southern Lakes region of New Zealand.

“Basically, all you have to do is show up on a trip. You don’t have to think about anything,” says CEO and co-founder Andy Levine. These active, planned trips inherently encourage people to unplug and make lasting connections with their fellow cyclists. “[We act as] their concierge, and we get to make these really cool things happen, all while on a bike.”

Then there’s Selar, a sustainable polar expedition company launching in early 2027. For anyone seeking a grand adventure—without all the intricate planning that often goes into these types of trips—Selar offers a chance to take a journey way up north with itineraries across Norway, Svalbard, and Greenland. Or take HX Expeditions, which, similar to a cruise model, offers all-inclusive voyages that cover excursions, meals, drinks, and gratuities—but unlike a classic cruise, these itineraries are led by onboard naturalists and historians, and their packages also include lectures, hands-on experiences, and expedition gear.

At Vermejo, a Ted Turner Reserve in New Mexico, each guest’s nature-focused itinerary is completely handled by their Reserve Ambassadors. There are more than 45 guided activities available, ranging from horseback riding through vast prairies, fly-fishing for Rio Grande cutthroat trout, or taking a UTV tour with the opportunity to spot elk, bear, and other big game.

Of course, numerous wellness resorts have been hopping on this trend for some time now, acknowledging that a true mental reset is intrinsically linked to less decision-making. BodyHoliday, an all-inclusive wellness resort in St. Lucia, asks guests to “give us your body for a week, and we’ll give you back your mind;” Sensei Lanai in Hawaii offers guided stays that cater to your individual health needs and goals; and at Renew Retreat at Calamigos Ranch in California, you can take part in a preset wellness retreat.

There are also a number of retreats specifically designed for staying active. Take a running retreat with Run Wild, for example: these women-only trips are designed so you simply choose your pace and place of interest and select an already-set itinerary.

The crux of these trips is rooted in analog experiences, which can help with the mental fatigue and technology overload we experience in everyday life. “I think people are really craving that opportunity to be in the moment, to be human,” says Crawford.

Surrendering complete control is never easy—but in the case of my trip to Santa Cruz, I found it wildly therapeutic. By not stressing about every detail, both before and during the trip, I was able to feel fully present with every item on my itinerary, including a hike through redwood forests, idly reading by the pool in my (pre-reserved) cabana, and savoring meals set to stunning views of the treetops and ocean beyond.

Because I opted for a road trip rather than air travel, the Pack Up + Go team even chose my rest stop on the way from Los Angeles (Paso Robles), along with recommendations for restaurants and shops to explore in the area. And this detailed research extended to my final destination in Santa Cruz, too: the team provided a meticulously curated list of shops, experiences, and more for me to explore during my final day in the area.

Before embarking on your own decision detox trip, take stock of whether this will feel freeing or cause you more stress. “My advice would be to reflect on whether you’re somebody who can actually let go of control,” says Cutlip. “If you’re going to turn it over to someone else and then micromanage the process, just do it yourself. If you can turn over control, fully embrace it and enjoy it.”

For me, it was a practice in letting go of control and allowing outside help—something I think many of us could benefit from on occasion. And while I won’t necessarily be handing over the reins for every future trip I take, it was a beautiful opportunity to give my mind a bit of reprieve, to stop overthinking for a while, and simply take in the wonder of a beautiful place.

COP31 Leaders’ Summit Spotlights Electrification & Transition Away from Fossil Fuels

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Antalya, Türkiye — The COP31 Leaders’ Summit concluded today, with several new announcements and speeches from heads of state and the UN Secretary-General. Developments included country endorsements of the COP Presidency’s 35×35 electrification pledge, growing support for the transition away from fossil fuels, and new pledges from philanthropies to the Tropical Forest Forever Facility, among others.

Following is a statement from Ani Dasgupta, President and CEO, World Resources Institute:

“One message came across loud and clear from world leaders: climate action has shifted from an environmental movement to a core economic and security imperative. The conversation is moving beyond high-level commitments to the practical challenges of how to transition away from fossil fuels while supporting workers and communities and how to put entire economies on a cleaner, more resilient path.

“Electrification and the transition away from fossil fuels rightly took center stage. These are two vital, intertwined goals, and progress on one must reinforce the other. Early support for the 35×35 electrification pledge is encouraging, but many more countries need to endorse it and turn their commitments into credible national plans backed by concrete policies and investment.

“President Lula deserves credit for keeping the transition away from fossil fuels at the top of the international agenda and delivering a global roadmap that countries can adapt to their own circumstances. Australia and Türkiye now have an opportunity to lead by example, putting forward their own transition plans and encouraging others to follow.

“Vulnerable countries delivered an equally clear message: access to affordable finance is critical to them implementing their climate ambition. High borrowing costs and unsustainable debt are holding back investment in the very countries that need it most. With the $1.3 trillion finance goal established, the focus is shifting to how all sources of finance can work together to deliver investment at scale and how we ensure affordable capital reaches those who need it most. There are encouraging signs, including an influx of pledges from philanthropies for the Brazil-initiated Tropical Forests Forever Facility.

“Adaptation and early warning systems received much-needed attention after a year of crushing heatwaves, fires, and floods. But that attention now needs to turn to the investment in systemic resilience as the best way to make economies and societies stronger and improve people’s lives. Building more resilient cities was a major focus and will take deep collaboration between national governments, cities, and states. Cities are on the front lines of delivering the climate solutions and essential services that make people’s lives better, and demonstrating these benefits is critical to accelerating the transition. Local leaders are on the front lines of climate impacts and the delivery of essential services that make communities safer, healthier, and more resilient, yet too often lack the resources to respond. Cities and local communities especially need greater access to adaptation finance.

“It was disappointing to see no dedicated ocean event at the Leaders’ Summit, despite over 90% of countries including ocean action in national climate plans. The ocean is on the front lines of the climate crisis, but it could also be one of our greatest allies in tackling it. Ocean-based solutions can provide up to one-third of the emissions cuts needed by 2050 to keep 1.5°C within reach. This ambition for ocean action must be reflected in any COP31 cover decision and translate into concrete progress, especially for vulnerable coastal and island nations.

“Some leaders also delivered a resounding defense of multilateralism, even amid deep geopolitical tensions. International cooperation on climate may be changing shape, but it is still alive and needed now more than ever. Even in a fractured world, leaders recognize that no country can secure its economic future, build resilience, or prosper without working together on shared climate and environmental challenges. That spirit of cooperation must now carry through the negotiations and deliver a strong, ambitious outcome at COP31.”

WRI is a global research organization that spans more than 50 countries, with offices in Brazil, China, Europe, India, Indonesia, and the United States. Our more than 450 experts and staff work closely with leaders to turn big ideas into action to sustain our natural resources—the foundation of economic opportunity and human well-being. Find out more at www.wri.org

Grazing sheep in fields with solar arrays

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Two new research papers shed light on the growing practice of grazing sheep in fields with solar arrays. One demonstrates that, in the right numbers, sheep grazing increases populations of beneficial insects like bees and lady beetles compared to mowing around solar panels. The other research describes a decision-making support tool to help farmers profitably grow their solar grazing herds.

A team from the College of Agriculture and Life Sciences (CALS) found that when two sheep per hectare graze around solar arrays, the abundance of wild bees, honey bees and lady beetles jumped by 38%, compared to a control plot that was mowed. The increase was driven primarily by an 83% increase in honey bees alone, according to the team’s findings in “Low solar-grazing densities support beneficial insects in agrivoltaic systems,” published Oct. 7 in Environmental Research Letters.

“Sheep grazing and beneficial insect habitat are two prominent conversation topics around solar development right now,” said Leana Zoller, the paper’s first author and a postdoctoral associate in natural resources in the Cornell CALS Ashley School of Global Development and the Environment. “But what’s been missing is research that treats them as parts of the same system. When we talk about sustainable solar development, integrating these discussions into a single framework is really helpful to farmers and community decision makers.”

For three years, Zoller and a team of entomologists, ecologists and animal and plant scientists tested various stocking rates – how many sheep per hectare are allowed to graze a certain plot of land – using Cornell’s teaching sheep grazing at the university-owned Cascadilla Community Solar Farm in Dryden, New York. At four sheep per hectare, they found beneficial insect abundance was similar to the mowed control site, while vegetation management effectiveness was greater than at two sheep per hectare. At eight sheep per hectare, vegetation management was maximized and sheep flock health remained high, but honey bee abundance dropped by 45%.

“We zeroed in on stocking rates because that’s a concrete number that sheep farmers understand and work with every day, and we want this research to be useful,” said Steve Grodsky, paper senior author and assistant professor of natural resources in the Ashley School. “With solar grazing, people, energy, agricultural animals, plants and insects are all part of this ecosystem. We’re trying to understand the connections between those pieces so that when solar developers, municipalities looking at solar development, or farmers are faced with a decision, they can have scientific information about potential trade-offs and synergies.”

Grodsky is also assistant unit leader of the U.S. Geological Survey New York Cooperative Fish and Wildlife Research Unit. Additional co-authors are sheep farmer and solar grazing consultant Nikola Kochendoerfer, Ph.D. ’20; Antonio DiTommaso, professor in the School of Integrative Plant Science; John Losey, professor of entomology; Scott McArt, Ph.D. ’12, associate professor of entomology; Paige Muñiz, formerly a technician in McArt’s lab; and Todd Ugine, Ph.D. ’04, research associate in Losey’s lab, all in CALS. Sarah Carson, director of Cornell’s Campus Sustainability Office, was key in coordinating site access between researchers and the solar company that manages the Cascadilla solar farm.

Another Cornell research team, from the Cornell SC Johnson College of Business, has developed a tool that helps farmers whose sheep graze in solar arrays to make better decisions about their operation. The team described the tool in “Flock Management for Solar Grazing,” which published Sept. 28 in Manufacturing & Service Operations Management.

After a chance encounter with Caleb Scott, Ithaca-based sheep farmer and founder of United Agrivoltaics, Vincent Slaugh, assistant professor at the Cornell Peter and Stephanie Nolan School of Hotel Administration and first author of the paper, began thinking through the economics and logistics of solar grazing. He recruited Linwei Xin, associate professor of operations research and information engineering, and Shane Henderson, Charles W. Lake, Jr. Professor in Productivity, both in the Cornell Duffield College of Engineering, to work on the topic.

Together, they developed a spreadsheet-based model that helps sheep farmers to optimize procurement and sales decisions, based on factors like target flock size, costs, anticipated revenues, and expected vegetation in particular solar sites; if a farmer doesn’t have enough sheep to keep the grass down, they’ll have to cut it some other way. It also helps solar developers understand growers’ needs and develop contracts that are more profitable and sustainable for all parties long-term. United Agrivoltaics has already incorporated the tool into its onboarding process.

Slaugh sees opportunity for U.S. shepherds to grow their operations – especially with solar contracts offering a steady source of income and animal feed. Current domestic sheep production for meat and wool doesn’t come close to meeting domestic demand; about half of all lamb and mutton consumed in the U.S. is imported, primarily from New Zealand and Australia.

“If we grow the sheep industry, will the American diet eventually include more lamb? Could that be a side effect of the solar energy revolution? I think the answer is very likely yes,” Slaugh said. “And then when that happens, we’d expect processors and supporting industries to come back, for banks to be willing to give more loans, and for supply chains to follow. All of that grows the economy, and rural economies in particular.”

Kochendoerfer, who grazes 700 ewes on solar installations in New York and Vermont, said that economy is already growing.

“I work with a lot of farms that are now scaling up because of solar,” Kochendoerfer said. “There is a new wave of sheep farmers who are capitalizing on this access to good land. With solar grazing, you get paid to graze as opposed to having to pay to graze. That leads to sustainable, meaningful economic income that these farmers can use to expand, build a barn, buy equipment or hire someone. It’s enabling a lot of folks to build sound, viable agricultural businesses.”

Funding for these research projects came from the Cornell Atkinson Center for Sustainability, the National Institute for Food and Agriculture and the National Science Foundation.

Krisy Gashler is a writer for the College of Agriculture and Life Sciences.

Microsoft fixes Windows activation as admins pick through Exchange outage wreckage

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Another unscheduled patch for IT teams already having quite enough fun with Redmond

Microsoft has released an out-of-band update to fix licensing and activation errors introduced by November's Windows security update.

The fix is available from the Microsoft Update Catalog for Windows Server 2025, 2022, and 2019, Windows 10 22H2 ESU, and Windows 10 Enterprise LTSC 2021 and 2019. Windows 11 23H2 users can obtain it from the Catalog or through Windows Update's Optional updates menu.

The "non-security OOB update" targets affected enterprise devices. Microsoft stated: "Certain Windows licensing and activation operations using the Software Licensing Management Tool (SLMGR) might fail with error 0x80041008 (Invalid parameter).

"This primarily affects organizations using slmgr.vbs commands or automated processes to configure Key Management Service (KMS) activation, install product keys, or manage Windows licensing."

Devices that are already activated will remain activated, Microsoft said.

Breaking the machinery customers use to activate paid-for software is a bad look for a Patch Tuesday release. Administrators may wonder how Microsoft's updates are tested when installing one means waiting for another to repair the damage.

Those same administrators may also have been fielding calls about last week's widespread Exchange Online outage, which Microsoft has partly attributed to a defect it introduced in July.

On top of that, Microsoft told administrators just days ago to consider unsupported Exchange Server 2016 and 2019 "permanently vulnerable." Exchange Online avoids that particular support deadline, but the recent outage was a reminder that handing over the servers doesn't make software defects disappear.

Forget "security by obscurity." Last week's approach was more like "security by unavailability."

Between getting Windows activated and keeping Exchange Online online, Microsoft gave administrators plenty to do. None of it was likely to feature in their original plans for the week.

Not all the glitches introduced by November's Patch Tuesday have yet been resolved, as some customers using a Windows device to print will be aware. ®

"There should be much less nullification happening now"

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

World of Warcraft: Forever has suffered from a dungeon experience-shaped problem since its Beta period, and Blizzard is well aware of fans' thoughts regarding it – so much so, in fact, that its latest hotfixes attempt to remedy some of the negative feedback.

In case you're out of the loop, dungeon XP in World of Warcraft: Forever has been a hot topic for quite some time now thanks to Blizzard's nerfs. The studio has been listening, though, and recently introduced a 50% boost to the experience points folks gain from kills in dungeons – but the changes didn't end there. Developers went on to nullify all XP gained from an enemy in a dungeon should a player's level be too high. It wasn't an entirely welcome change, to put things lightly, however, leading to yet another quick tweak from Blizzard. Associate production director Clay Stone explains as much over on Twitter, writing, "We just made a change to ease the recent restrictions on XP earned from enemies in dungeons in WoW: Forever. There should be much less nullification happening now." He links to the official blog post on said change.

We just made a change to ease the recent restrictions on XP earned from enemies in dungeons in WoW Forever. There should be much less nullification happening now. Thanks for the feedback, all! Full blue post here:November 15, 2026

"Yesterday, we updated dungeon play so that experience granted by an enemy in an instance is nullified if a high-level player is in the group and the enemy is trivial to that player," it reads. "For some dungeons, the new restriction missed the mark because of the overall level range of the enemies in the dungeon." That's why, as Blizzard goes on to state, new hotfixes have been implemented to loosen this restriction a bit.

"With hotfixes that are now live, we've just deployed a further change to this restriction, making it so that the XP nullification only kicks in when the high-level player is more than 12 levels above the level of the enemies," the devs conclude – and, unsurprisingly, fans are pretty happy to hear it. "I think this was the only thing needed to make this change fantastic," one comment under the forum post asserts. " +12 levels is enough to curtail boosting, but not enough to impact players normally trying to compete quests in the megadungeons." Another adds, "Thank you. That’s more reasonable." The consensus seems to largely be the same on other discussion boards online, too, like those on Reddit, where somebody jokes, "Alright guys lets find something else to complain about now." In other words, GG, Blizzard – this one's going over well.

As for what the future of dungeons and the grind for XP in World of Warcraft: Forever will look like… well, that remains to be seen. I'm pretty content with questing away to get my Druid a bit higher as it stands now, but I'm excited to see what Blizzard's still got in store.

Ask Dr. Paola - HELP! What Does It Mean When a Dog’s Hackles Go Up in Different Places? (November 16, 2026)

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Health  |  Read Source →

We just sent a welcome email your way with a quick overview of what to expect (including those vital recall alerts).

Dogster is reader-supported. When you buy via links on our site, we may earn a commission at no cost to you. Read about privacy.

Welcome to our "Ask Dr. Paola" series, where every other Monday we bring expert advice straight from Dr. Paola Cuevas (MVZ) to help our readers better understand their dog's health and well-being.

Whether you're a new pet parent or a seasoned dog lover, Dr. Paola is here to provide answers to your most pressing questions. From nutrition tips and preventive care to troubleshooting common behavioral issues, Dr. Paola is ready to offer insights that will keep your furry friend happy and healthy. Stay tuned for expert guidance on a range of topics that matter most to you and your dog, so you can make informed decisions and provide the best possible care for your canine companion.

âHaveâ a âquestion? Send it in here!

I have a question about my dog, Jethro. Sometimes when he alerts, the hackles between his shoulders rise. Other times they are prominent along his lower back. What does each appearance indicate about how he is responding?â

That is a great observation! When a dogâs hackles rise, it is an involuntary response controlled by the autonomic nervous system called piloerection, and it tells us that his arousal level has increased. However, the location of the raised hair does not reliably tell us which emotion a dog is experiencing.

When the hackles rise between his shoulders, it can accompany alertness, excitement, uncertainty, or concern. When they appear along his lower back, the same range of emotional responses is possible. Neither pattern automatically indicates aggression, fear, or a particular level of threat. Think of hackles as a little emotional alarm bell; they tell us Jethro is experiencing heightened arousal, but not exactly why.

Different dogs will respond to different stimuli in different ways, so thereâs no âone size fits allâ explanation for why different areas are triggered. I would pay close attention to what is happening around him when this occurs. Is he alerting to a stranger approaching the house, another dog walking past, an unfamiliar noise, or something unexpected in the environment? You might want to start keeping a record of what is going on before and after Jethroâs hackles are raised, and whether itâs his shoulders or lower back that is involved.

Combine that context with his facial expression, ear position, tail carriage, posture, muscle tension, and ability to disengage. Looking at the whole picture helps us understand whether Jethro is curious, excited, uncertain, or uncomfortable, and, most importantly, how we can best support him. These details, together with what triggered the alert, give us a much clearer picture of how Jethro is feeling.

She received her degree from the University of Guadalajara, Mexico.

Half-Life 2 writer's biggest regret after 12 years at Valve is not making fun of Gabe Newell's shirt

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Former Valve developer Chet Faliszek – writer behind the likes of Half-Life 2, Portal and its sequel, Team Fortress 2, Left 4 Dead, and even Counter-Strike: Global Offensive – has one big regret about his time at the Steam company: a joke he didn't make.

Yes, you read that right. He reveals as much in a new short over on YouTube, asking, "What's the thing I regret the most at Valve? I was there for 12 years. So, what's the thing that I regret, that I did or didn't do?" Apparently, after sitting on this question for some time, he's become brave enough to publicly share his answer – an answer he thinks of "almost every year," as it weighs that heavy in his mind. Brace yourselves for this recollection, y'all.

"If you remember, Valve had Steam Dev Days. We actually had a really early Steam Dev Days where I spoke at, and I spoke horribly, and I made a note that I've got to learn how to speak in public. I'm still not good at it, but it was just so bad. I was just shaking. It was just horrible. So, at this point, when Steam Dev Days two comes around, I'm like, 'Hey, I'll help out.'" Valve then decided to make him the host.

The joke Faliszek mustered up was "going to put Gabe on the spot" – a joke about his polo shirt, to be precise. "My joke was, 'And here's Gabe Newell modeling the latest in polo,' because he always wore a polo shirt. 'Today's selection is polo after dark.'" The dev knew Newell would "lean into it" had he said it, but he kept it to himself and laughed "so hard" without ever, uh, actually making the joke out loud.

Apparently that's nothing new for him, as he's often "laughing at jokes I don't say out loud because I'm thinking, 'That's going to push it.'" He does, again, regret not making this one in particular, though. "I regret that. I should have said that joke. That was a good joke. I know it's not the greatest joke, but at the time with Gabe walking in his black polo, it would have worked." And, honestly, I'd have to agree.

To this day, I seldom ever see Newell not wearing a polo shirt… if ever. I hope he tunes into Faliszek's channel and hears the joke, however, even if it's a good few years after it should've been spoken out loud.

Epic Games' Tim Sweeney says Steam's AI disclosure is "unfair to developers who use AI to make good games" because it associates AI "with bad games."

She has written for various publications since her postgraduate studies, including Dexerto, Fanbyte, GameSpot, IGN, PCGamesN, and more. When she's not frantically trying to form words into coherent sentences, she's probably daydreaming about becoming a fairy druid and befriending every animal or she's spending a thousand (more) hours traversing the Underdark in Baldur's Gate 3. If you spot her away from her PC, you'll always find Anna with a fantasy book, a handheld video game console of some sort, and a Tamagotchi or two on hand.

Robert Pattinson cast as next James Bond

📅 2026-11-16  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Robert Pattinson has been cast as the next James Bond in the upcoming film from director Denis Villeneuve. It will be the 26th James Bond film and the first since Amazon MGM took over the iconic franchise.

Pattinson will be the seventh actor to play the role. Monday's announcement — which came in part via an Instagram post — ends a long period of speculation about who would take over as "007" after Daniel Craig.

"James Bond is one of the most legendary characters in cinema, and to be entrusted with 007 is the honour of a lifetime," Pattinson said in a statement. "I couldn't be more excited about this partnership and the work ahead with this fantastic team."

Pattinson has appeared in several big-ticket films this year, including Christopher Nolan's "The Odyssey" and psychological thriller "Primetime," and he'll soon appear in "Dune: Part Three," also directed by Villeneuve. Pattinson, who rose to fame with the Twilight movie series, is also in production for Matt Reeves' "The Batman Part II," a follow-up to the 2022 film based on the DC comic.

The upcoming Bond film will have a script by Steven Knight and will be produced by Amy Pascal, known for her work on the Spider-Man franchise, and David Heyman, who produced all eight Harry Potter films.

"We were surrounded by extraordinary talent, but there can only be one 007. I am overjoyed to welcome Robert Pattinson as our new James Bond. He has the timeless soul of 007 and brings a spirit that is completely new. I cannot wait to begin," Villeneuve said.

In 2022, Amazon bought MGM Studios, which had long produced the Bond movies, for roughly $8.45 billion. Amazon MGM then acquired creative control of the James Bond intellectual property rights in 2025, when it closed a deal with longtime producers Barbara Broccoli and Michael G. Wilson.

The film is still untitled, and Amazon MGM hasn't announced a projected release date.

Ask Dr. Paola – HELP! My Senior Cat Won’t Leave the Bedroom (November 16, 2026)

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Health  |  Read Source →

Catster is reader-supported. When you buy via links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission at no cost to you. Learn more.

Welcome to our "Ask Dr. Paola" series, where every other Monday we bring expert advice straight from Dr. Paola Cuevas (MVZ) to help our readers better understand their cat's health and well-being. Whether you're a new pet parent or a seasoned cat lover, Dr. Paola is here to provide answers to your most pressing questions. From nutrition tips and preventive care to troubleshooting common behavioral issues, Dr. Paola is ready to offer insights that will keep your kitty happy, healthy, and feline fine. Stay tuned for expert guidance on a range of topics that matter most to you and your cat, so you can make informed decisions and provide the best possible care for your furry companion. â

My cat, Mitzi, is 16 years and 8 months old. She has early-stage kidney disease and hyperthyroidism, which she is on medication for. About 2 months ago, she became deaf, and since then has refused to leave the master bedroom. I have moved her water bowl and litter box there and feed her there.

Is there anything I can do to get her to come out?â

At her age, sweet Mitzi has certainly earned the right to enjoy her golden years at her own pace, but I can imagine it is difficult to see her withdraw from the rest of the house, especially when you remember how she used to move around. However, I would not necessarily interpret her reluctance to leave the bedroom as something we need to correct. Losing her hearing can be quite disorienting. Imagine walking into a room where you can no longer hear footsteps, familiar voices, or the little sounds that help you understand what is happening around you. For Mitzi, the bedroom may now feel like her safest little world, where everything is familiar and predictable.

I actually think you have done a wonderful job adapting her environment by bringing her food, water, and litter box closer. Rather than encouraging her to leave right away, I would focus on helping her feel confident enough to explore if she wishes. You could start by sitting with her near the bedroom doorway, offering a favorite treat or a little affection. Gradually place treats a short distance beyond the door, allowing her to decide whether she wants to investigate.

Take care to only approach her within her visual field, avoid startling her while she sleeps, and keep pathways clear and nonslip. Think of it as extending her new world one comfortable step at a time, rather than asking her to return to her old routine.

There is one thing I would not overlook, though. Mitzi has both chronic kidney disease and hyperthyroidism, and at her age, reduced mobility or withdrawal can also be associated with arthritis, high blood pressure, visual changes, or inadequate control of her thyroid disease. I would recommend a senior cat veterinary checkup that includes blood pressure measurement, assessment for arthritis, and follow-up kidney and thyroid testing. Sometimes what looks like a behavioral change is actually a senior cat quietly telling us that they are in pain and something does not feel quite right.

Most importantly, please do not feel that you are encouraging a bad habit by keeping her essentials in the bedroom. You are adapting her home to meet her changing needs, and that is a beautiful expression of your love and care for her. If the bedroom is where Mitzi feels safe, eats well, drinks, uses her litter box, and enjoys your company, that can be a perfectly good quality of life.

At this stage, happiness is not measured by how much of the house she explores, but by how comfortable and secure she feels in the little world she chooses.

As a certified cat-lover, Radim was excited to join the Catster team on the mission to help all the cat parents in the world by providing useful and helpful information. He used to live with two wonderful cats before embarking on his travels around the world. Radim currently lives in Slovakia, surrounded by nature and his neighbors' cat community.

'We've Heard You!' - Star Wars Galactic Racer Is Officially Adding Split-Screen Multiplayer

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

An incredible addition to an incredible game

Star Wars Galactic Racer is my favourite game of the year so far. It's an incredible racing title that even surpasses the original Star Wars Episode 1 Racer in my view, and I've had so much fun with the campaign and online multiplayer.

There's just one downside that everyone's been pointing out since it was revealed, and that's the absence of local split-screen multiplayer. With that in mind, the development team have found a way to implement it in a new update.

The update isn't available yet, but will be coming to the game in early 2027. Alongside it will be a new mode called Free Play, which is where you'll be able to freely choose your vehicle, character, planet and race track.

"We’ve heard you! Local split-screen multiplayer racing is coming in early 2027. A dedicated team at Fuse Games has been working hard to bring a brand new mode, Free Play, to STAR WARS: Galactic Racer. This new mode will allow you to choose your vehicle, character, planet, and race track to race alone or with up to 3 other players in local split-screen action."

It seems Free Play is the only place where you'll be able to access local split-screen multiplayer in Star Wars Galactic Racer, but that's no surprise considering how solo-focused the main campaign is. They'd literally have to rewrite massive portions of the campaign if they were going to implement split-screen into it.

Anyway, this is just an amazing bonus on top of an already amazing game, and I encourage you to check it out if you enjoy a title like Burnout 3: Takedown - it's fast-paced and frantic, has the same kind of takedown system that you'll remember from that game, and the roguelite campaign is really well designed (online is great fun as well).

If you've played it, let us know what you think of the game in the poll down below!

ISC2 AI Security Academy Builds Cybersecurity Professionals' AI Security Skills

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Three learning tracks enable cybersecurity professionals to focus on their AI security responsibilities and professional goals

ALEXANDRIA, Va., Nov. 16, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- ISC2 â the world's leading nonprofit member organization for cybersecurity professionals â today launched the ISC2 AI Security Academy, a structured professional development framework designed to help cybersecurity professionals build practical capabilities for securing AI-enabled environments. The Academy features clear learning pathways â including certificates, courses and workshops â that are mapped to three learning tracks, each focused on a different set of AI security responsibilities.

As AI changes governance, risk management, security operations, software security and more, cybersecurity professionals need ongoing, role-relevant AI security learning to keep up with the pace of change. According to the latest ISC2 Cybersecurity Workforce Study, 41% of participants who said their organization's security team had at least one skill need cited AI skills as the most pressing need.

"Understanding AI's opportunities and risks is only a starting point. Professionals need relevant AI security learning they can put to work in their own roles," said Casey Marks, Ph.D., ISC2 Chief Operations Officer. "The Academy gives them a place to start that reflects their responsibilities and experience. Rather than following the same path, cybersecurity professionals can tailor their AI security development to build on what they have already learned and continue developing skills as roles evolve."

Three Tracks of Practical AI Security Training

The framework supports a shared understanding of AI security while providing increasing depth aligned to professional responsibility and decision authority, covering the following topics:

New learning opportunities are continually added to the Academy's portfolio, giving cybersecurity practitioners and leaders ongoing opportunities to add to their skills base and expertise.

A Commitment to Skilling the Cybersecurity Workforce for AI

The Academy is an important step in ISC2's commitment to give its members and the broader cybersecurity community the resources and skills they need to succeed in the age of AI. Over the past year, ISC2 has expanded the courses, workshops, webinars and research available to its members.

Earlier this year, ISC2 also released Exam Guidance for Artificial Intelligence to show how AI-related skills connect to its certifications. ISC2 is also in the final phase of developing an AI security certification, shaped by nearly 12,000 volunteers worldwide. The pilot exam outline is now available, and 3,000 volunteers will be invited to take the pilot exam later this year. Alongside these efforts, ISC2 has established the Cyber-AI Workforce Coalition to define how cybersecurity roles will evolve in the AI era.

For more information, visit the ISC2 AI Security Academy.

ISC2 is the world's leading member organization for cybersecurity professionals, driven by our vision of a safe and secure cyber world. Our more than 270,000 certified members, and associates, are a force for good, safeguarding the way we live. Our award-winning certifications â including cybersecurity's premier certification, the CISSPÂ® â enable professionals to demonstrate their knowledge, skills and abilities at every stage of their careers. ISC2 strengthens the influence, diversity and vitality of the cybersecurity profession through advocacy, expertise and workforce empowerment that accelerates cyber safety and security in an interconnected world. Our charitable foundation, the Center for Cyber Safety and Education, helps create more access to cyber careers and educates those most vulnerable. Carlos Alcaraz powered past Frances ‌Tiafoe 6-2 6-2 in just over an hour at the ATP ‌Finals on Monday to stretch his winning run to ​10 matches.

The Spaniard is aiming for his third straight title after lifting trophies in Vienna and Paris.

He's also aiming to claim the title at the season-ending tournament for the first time after losing last year's championship match in Turin to Jannik Sinner .

Sinner, the two-time defending champion at the ATP Finals, ended his season early due to a right knee injury.

Tiafoe's baseline game was no match for Alcaraz's power and movement, with ​the world No 3 dominant from ‌the start.

Alcaraz has won three titles since returning to the tour at the US Open after a ​break of nearly five months due ⁠to a wrist injury.

The 2025 ⁠finalist quickly charged into a 4-0 lead as the American world ‌No 8 was left looking helpless against Alcaraz's versatile play.

In a near-flawless first set, ‌the Spaniard won 93 per cent of points on his first serve and a perfect 100 per cent on his second.

Before Tiafoe, making his ATP Finals debut, had time ⁠to react he was down a set ​and a break after half an ​hour.

"He is right because the calendar is so congested. The move to 12-day Masters events means players are away more, it's more mentally demanding - maybe more so than physical and he feels like he doesn't have to play every week of the year.

"He's going to decide what the biggest tournaments are for him and then he's going to try and peak for them."

Napoli, fatturato da record: 384 milioni. Tutte le cifre tra incassi e spese del bilancio 2025/2026

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Chiude con un bilancio utile la SSC Napoli dopo la perdita registrata nell’esercizio precedente. Il ritorno in Champions League, il peso delle operazioni di mercato, il risarcimento ottenuto nell’ambito del contenzioso con IMG e lo sviluppo del comparto commerciale hanno permesso alla società partenopea di migliorare i propri risultati economici, a fronte di una crescita altrettanto significativa dei costi.

Stando ai documenti ufficiali, il Napoli ha archiviato l’esercizio al 30 giugno 2026 con un valore della produzione di 384,3 milioni di euro, rispetto ai circa 291 milioni dell’annata precedente. E’ un primato assoluto nella storia della società di Aurelio De Laurentiis, che supera anche i 359,2 milioni contabilizzati nel 2022/2023, stagione culminata con la conquista del terzo scudetto. Le entrate derivanti dai diritti televisivi hanno raggiunto quota 133 milioni di euro, contro i 78,1 milioni della stagione 2024/2025. Nello specifico, gli introiti provenienti dalle competizioni UEFA sono cresciuti da 3,9 a 49,5 milioni di euro, ai quali si aggiungono quelli legati alla partecipazione alla Supercoppa italiana. Le plusvalenze derivanti dalle cessioni dei calciatori si sono attestate intorno ai 102,7 milioni di euro. L’operazione più remunerativa è stata quella riguardante Victor Osimhen, che ha assicurato al club una plusvalenza di circa 73,2 milioni di euro. Tra le altre cessioni più significative ci sono quelle di Giacomo Raspadori, Alessandro Zanoli e Giovanni Simeone. I trasferimenti a titolo temporaneo hanno inoltre generato proventi per circa 8,7 milioni di euro. Nel dettaglio, ecco come si compongono i ricavi della SSC Napoli: Ricavi da gare: 31,3 milioni di euro (24,1 milioni nel 2024/2025); Ricavi da sponsorizzazioni: 52,9 milioni (48,1 milioni); Ricavi commerciali e royalties: 25,5 milioni (20,1 milioni); Proventi vari: 1,7 milioni (1,3 milioni); Proventi da cessione dei diritti televisivi: 133 milioni (78,1 milioni); Plusvalenze da cessione dei calciatori: 102,7 milioni (102,6 milioni); Altri ricavi e proventi diversi: 36,8 milioni (16,6 milioni); Contributi in conto esercizio: 500mila euro (0 euro); Totale: 384,3 milioni (290,9 milioni). Nella voce relativa agli altri proventi rientra anche un’entrata straordinaria derivante dall’accordo transattivo raggiunto dalla Lega Serie A nell’ambito della controversia con IMG per i diritti audiovisivi internazionali. L’intesa ha garantito alla società azzurra un introito di 19,3 milioni di euro.

Nell’esercizio chiuso al 30 giugno 2022, il Napoli aveva contabilizzato 12,1 milioni di euro di ricavi da gara e ulteriori 37,2 milioni provenienti da sponsorizzazioni, attività commerciali e royalties, per un totale di 49,3 milioni. Nella stagione 2025/2026, queste stesse voci hanno raggiunto complessivamente 109,7 milioni di euro, di cui: 31,3 milioni derivanti dal matchday, 52,9 milioni dalle sponsorizzazioni e 25,5 milioni dalle attività commerciali e dalle royalties.

Aumentano anche i costi di gestione. I costi della produzione hanno raggiunto quota 373,3 milioni di euro, rispetto ai 315,5 milioni dell’esercizio precedente, con una crescita di circa il 18%. Di seguito il dettaglio delle principali voci di costo del bilancio azzurro:

-Materie prime, sussidiarie, di consumo e merci: 10,4 milioni di euro (6,5 milioni nel 2024/2025);

-Costi per servizi: 30,5 milioni (36 milioni); Costi per godimento di beni di terzi: 8,1 milioni (6,7 milioni);

-Costi per il personale: 172,3 milioni (142,1 milioni), di cui 165,3 milioni per salari e stipendi (135,8 milioni);

-Ammortamenti e svalutazioni: 135 milioni (118,2 milioni);

-Variazione delle rimanenze: -800mila euro (-100mila euro);

-Oneri diversi di gestione: 17,7 milioni (6,2 milioni); Totale: 373,3 milioni (315,5 milioni).

A incidere maggiormente sull’aumento delle spese è stato il costo della squadra, anche in delle virtù operazioni sul mercato effettuate per soddisfare le richieste dell’ex allenatore Antonio Conte. Infatti, la voce salari è cresciuta dai 132,9 milioni dell’esercizio precedente ai 161,9 milioni contabilizzati al 30 giugno 2026. I soli compensi contrattuali dei calciatori hanno raggiunto quota 128,9 milioni di euro, ai quali si aggiungono i premi legati al conseguimento dei risultati sportivi, anch’essi in aumento.

Tuttavia, la crescita del fatturato ha permesso al Napoli di tornare a registrare un risultato economico positivo. La differenza tra il valore ei costi della produzione si è attestata intorno agli 11 milioni di euro, invertendo la tendenza negativa dell’esercizio precedente. Al netto della componente fiscale, il Napoli ha archiviato il bilancio al 30 giugno 2026 con un utile di circa 2,2 milioni di euro. Un risultato in netto miglioramento rispetto alla perdita di 21,4 milioni di euro registrata dodici mesi prima. Il club partenopeo torna quindi in utile dopo un solo anno caratterizzato da un risultato economico negativo, raggiungendo contestualmente il fatturato più elevato della propria storia.

Infine, sul documento contabile ci sono indicazioni significative anche sulla situazione patrimoniale e finanziaria della SSC Napoli. Il patrimonio netto si mantiene ampiamente positivo, attestandosi a circa 192,4 milioni di euro, mentre le disponibilità liquide ammontano a 172,9 milioni.
