Children’s brain responses to food may help predict later fat gain

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Food  |  Read Source →

Children who ate more ultra-processed foods showed stronger brain responses to food pictures, which were linked to greater body fat gain later.

Breakfast cereal, packaged bread and instant soup get blamed for childhood weight gain because of what’s in them.

A new study links children’s ultra-processed food intake to how their brains respond to pictures of food.

A year later, the children with stronger reactions had gained more body fat. Every child was a healthy weight when the study began, and this kind of study can show a link but not prove a cause.

Dr. Kathleen Page, a physician and professor of medicine and pediatrics at the Keck School of Medicine of USC, is the study’s senior author, and Jasmin Alves is the first author.

“Children’s brains are still developing, habits are still forming and food preferences are still being made. It’s an important period where the brain is very sensitive to food and the food environment,” Dr. Page said.

The 46 children were 7 to 10 years old and part of the BrainChild study.

That long-running project follows children whose mothers did or did not have diabetes during pregnancy, and it recruits them through Kaiser Permanente Southern California.

Each child had a BMI below the 85th percentile for their age and sex.

On two separate days, each child and a parent walked trained staff through everything the child had eaten in the previous 24 hours.

The researchers then sorted every food by how processed it was. On average, ultra-processed foods supplied about 68% of the children’s daily calories, close to the 67% a 2021 analysis found for American kids as a whole.

About a month later, each child lay in an MRI scanner after a 12-hour fast and then a glucose drink.

The scanner recorded how active two deep brain areas were during the food photos.

Both areas are part of a structure called the striatum, which handles motivation, learning and reward.

The ventral striatum is involved in anticipating and experiencing rewards. The dorsal striatum helps link cues with learned actions and habits.

Children who got more of their calories from ultra-processed foods had stronger dorsal striatum responses to the food photos.

The ventral striatum showed no clear pattern. The association with dorsal striatum responses remained after accounting for each child’s age, sex, body size and whether their mother had diabetes during pregnancy.

In an interview with Earth.com, Dr. Page emphasized that the association held even after the researchers accounted for total calorie intake, suggesting that how much children ate did not fully explain the pattern.

The children also consumed a standardized drink before their scans, “so the stronger response to food cues wasn’t simply due to hunger,” she said.

Rodent studies had already shown that a steady diet of sugar-rich food changes how the dorsal striatum works.

No earlier study had looked directly at how much ultra-processed food people habitually eat and how their striatum responds, the authors wrote.

The children came back for measurements roughly a year later, with an average gap of about 15 months.

Their BMI had risen by one point on average, their body fat by close to one percentage point, and their waists by about 1.5 inches (3.9 centimeters).

Children whose dorsal striatum had reacted more strongly to the food photos gained more in BMI and body fat. Eating more ultra-processed food was linked to a bigger waist a year later.

Ultra-processed food intake’s links to BMI and body fat were weak enough that chance could explain them.

The team then asked how much of the link between diet and fat gain involved the brain.

Once the dorsal striatum response was in the model, the association between ultra-processed food and body fat gain shrank by 44%, and the one with BMI by 31%.

Dr. Page said the result does more than repeat that these foods are tied to weight gain, because it points to a pathway in the brain.

“It actually gives us insight into the neurobiological underpinnings by which these foods may be linked to body composition over time.”

What stood out most to Dr. Page was that every child was a healthy weight when the study began.

“This suggests that the link between ultra-processed foods and the brain’s response to food cues may be detectable before obesity develops, rather than simply resulting from excess weight,” she told Earth.com.

Added sugar is in most ultra-processed foods, so the team checked whether sugar was driving the result.

Children who ate more added sugar tended to have stronger striatum responses too, but the pattern wasn’t clear enough to rule out chance.

When the team accounted for added sugar, the link between ultra-processed food and the dorsal striatum weakened and was no longer statistically clear.

Still, the authors wrote that sugar is unlikely to be the whole story.

Forty-six children is a small group, and the authors wrote that this limited their power to detect modest effects. Brain scans of this kind are also hard to reproduce in small groups.

Each child was scanned only once, so no one knows yet whether the stronger responses persist or fade as children get older.

An observational study like this one can’t say which came first. Children whose reward circuits already react more strongly to food may be the ones who reach for ultra-processed foods, rather than the other way around.

And because every child started at a healthy weight, the pattern may look different in children who already carry extra weight.

The research team said the burden shouldn’t fall only on families and children, because these foods are often the most accessible, affordable and convenient ones available.

“Changing the food environment to help people access healthier, more minimally processed foods is an important part of the solution,” Dr. Page noted.

For families in the meantime, Dr. Page said the practical goal is to shift the overall diet toward more minimally processed foods. “Parents should not feel that they have to eliminate every packaged food.”

Her examples were water instead of soda and fruit instead of fruit juice or fruit snacks. The aim is more vegetables, beans, nuts, eggs and other foods closer to their original form.

One way to test whether the diet is driving the brain response is to change the diet.

“A future intervention study could test whether replacing ultra-processed foods with mostly minimally processed foods changes how children’s brains respond to food cues and helps prevent excess body-fat gain,” Dr. Page said.

Meanwhile, the BrainChild team will keep following these children through puberty and into young adulthood.

Dr. Page said the next step is to see how their diets relate to changes in the developing brain. The team will also look at their risk for obesity over time.

The full study was published in the journal JAMA Network Open.

Ariana Grande Over the Moon About Her Four Grammy Nominations: ‘Such a Sweet and Unexpected Honor’

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

The singer snagged her first song of the year nod and seventh notice for best pop vocal album when nominations were announced on Monday (Nov. 16).

Ariana Grande woke up weeping for the very best reason on Monday morning (Nov. 16). The singer was celebrating her four Grammy nominations after this year’s list of nominees was announced, sharing her feelings in a lengthy Instagram post touting the good news. Explore Ariana Grande See latest videos, charts and news

“hellow, good day, crying !!!!!!!!!!! petal !!!!!!!!! i am so overwhelmed, it is hard to find words at the moment. but for now, simply… thank you so, so much @grammys for recognizing my sweet Petal and ‘Hate That I Made You Love Me in this way,'” she wrote. “i’m so irrevocably proud of this body of work and it is such an honor to receive this acknowledgment from you. ♡.” Related Westlife Sets 2027 North America Tour: Exclusive Lily Collins Pays Tribute to Phil Collins After Rock Hall Induction Watch Olivia Dean, Kehlani, Juanes & More Revealing 2027 Grammy Nominations Here

The celebration came after Grande landed her first song of the year nod for the single “hate that i made you love me,” as well as her record seventh nomination for best pop vocal album for her 8th studio LP, this year’s Petal. The nominations pushed Grande past Taylor Swift and Kelly Clarkson, who’ve each received six nominations in the latter category; Grande is also nominated for best traditional pop vocal performance for her Wicked: For Good collaboration “For Good” with Cynthia Erivo and best video of the year for “hate that i made you love me.”

Grande’s post also shared the love with her Petal collaborators, producers and “dear friends” Ilya Salmanzadeh and Max Martin. “i have loved every moment of doing this with you both, growing in so many new directions and sprouting new leaves together over the past 12 years…,” she said. “thank you for supporting my curiosity as an artist and for championing me as a songwriter and producer the way that you do. you two are the greatest there is in every way. ♡”

She also gave props to director Christian Breslauer, who helmed her “hate that I made you love me” visual and for “being able to bring these songs to life in a way that only you can. thank you for caring the way you do and for taking the time to make sure that every single frame of each video we do together shines a light on the truth of each song. it’s an honor to create with you every time. ♡”

Grande, who announced that she’s taking a break from music after wrapping up her Eternal Sunshine tour in September, concluded her post with a summary of how important Petal was and is to her. “everything about creating and releasing this project has been such a meaningful experience for me already and this acknowledgment is such a sweet and unexpected honor and an extraordinary addition to that,” she said. “so thank you, thank you, again… petal forever. ♡”

Breslauer appreciated the shout-out, commenting, “🥹🥹🫶🏻 congrats and thank you my brilliant friend!”

To date, Grande has racked up 24 Grammy nominations and three wins, including best pop vocal album in 2019 for Sweetener, best pop duo/group performance in 2021 for “Rain on Me” with Lady Gaga and best pop duo/group performance in 2026 for “Defying Gravity” with Cynthia Erivo.

Her four nods this year come during a Grammys season when women are the dominant nominees, with RAYE leading the pack with 11 nominations, followed by Rosalía with nine nominations and Phoebe Bridgers, Miranda Lambert, Ella Langley and Olivia Rodrigo with seven each.

The 2027 Grammy Awards will take place on Feb. 7, with the prime-time broadcast airing at 7 p.m. ET/4 p.m. PT on ABC and streaming live on Disney+ and Hulu; the Premiere Ceremony, where the majority of the 100 prizes will be handed out, will take place earlier in the day.

US Could ‘100%’ Lose Crypto Race To China, Digital Chamber CEO Warns

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Log in to access your notifications and stay updated. If you’re not a member yet, Sign Up to get started!

Writer by choice, CryptoCurrency Writer, and Researcher by chance. Currently, focusing on financial news and analysis, as well as cryptocurrency news and data. One may not call me a crypto “Enthusiast” but trust me I'm getting there.

Writer by choice, CryptoCurrency Writer, and Researcher by chance. Currently, focusing on financial news and analysis, as well as cryptocurrency news and data. One may not call me a crypto “Enthusiast” but trust me I'm getting there.

Writer by choice, CryptoCurrency Writer, and Researcher by chance. Currently, focusing on financial news and analysis, as well as cryptocurrency news and data. One may not call me a crypto “Enthusiast” but trust me I'm getting there.

The crypto industry’s run of friendly treatment in Washington looks set to end. Cody Carbone, chief executive of The Digital Chamber, warned that a Democrat-controlled Congress will be “very hostile” to digital assets. Broad market structure legislation such as the CLARITY Act is now unlikely to pass.

Lawmakers are instead expected to focus on investigating crypto companies with ties to President Donald Trump. The political shift comes as Bitcoin trades at its lowest level since July.

Carbone said in an interview that the industry is unlikely to get the broad legislation it had pushed for. The CLARITY Act, meant to set clear rules for how digital assets are regulated in the US, has been left in limbo.

Two longtime critics of the industry are set to gain influence over financial policy: Senator Elizabeth Warren in the Senate and Representative Maxine Waters in the House.

For crypto firms, the agenda in Washington is moving from passing rules to defending against scrutiny.

Trump’s Crypto Ties Move Into the Spotlight

The industry is preparing for sweeping inquiries into crypto businesses connected to the President. Carbone expects Warren and Waters to press hard on Trump’s links to digital assets.

The Digital Chamber’s plan is to counter those investigations by shifting the debate to the technology itself. Carbone wants supportive lawmakers to steer hearings toward topics such as tokenization, so the conversation is about substance rather than politics.

The strategy depends on finding allies inside the incoming majority. Carbone admitted that pro-crypto Democrats are “few and far between,” but said their numbers are growing.

Many of the newly elected Democrats are young and focused on technology. According to Carbone, several have told the industry they are “crypto curious.”

The Digital Chamber plans to target moderates and junior committee members. Carbone said no single Democrat can take on Warren alone, but a larger group could. The aim is to build enough support within the party to push back against the more hostile voices.

Opposition to data centers poses another major threat, Carbone said. Local resistance to energy-heavy facilities hits both crypto mining and artificial intelligence.

Carbone said voters made their concerns clear in the November election and have a right to be skeptical. Still, the industry argues that bans and moratoriums are the wrong response.

The Digital Chamber plans to show lawmakers how data centers can bring jobs and revenue to struggling districts. Carbone described blockchain, digital assets and AI as closely linked industries, and framed leadership in all three as a matter of national competitiveness.

The big warning was about America’s global position. Asked whether the US could lose its lead in the sector within the next two years, Carbone answered and said “100%.”

Developers in crypto and AI are already moving to friendlier places such as the UAE and Singapore. Carbone pointed to China, which holds the most blockchain patents worldwide, as the country most likely to take the lead if the US falls behind.

“If we don’t lead, other adversaries will,” Carbone said, arguing that the US must set the global rules before its rivals do.

NASA's Voyager 1 reaches a light-day away from Earth

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Science  |  Read Source →

When just saying 'hello' takes a two-day round trip, you're a light-day, or 26 billion kilometers, from Earth. Only one human-made object has ever traveled that far — and its journey into interstellar space continues.

Hate long-haul flights? Well, imagine having to travel 3,300 years to get to your destination.

Imagine departing in 1977 and arriving in 2026, on November 18 at 11:16 a.m. Central European Time to be precise, and then, not stopping but continuing the journey.

NASA's Voyager 1, a spacecraft launched almost half-a-century ago, has traveled a light-day from Earth.

A light-day is the distance light covers in 24 hours — about 26 billion kilometers (or around 16 billion miles). Put another way: Light does in one day what it took the spacecraft 49 years, two months and 11 days to do.

But Voyager 1 is not only far away, it is also traveling at an incredible speed of 61,000 km/h (38,000 mph).

When Voyager 1 launched, it was in sync with a planetary alignment that was as rare as the spacecraft itself — one that happens only once every 176 years. It was the alignment Jupiter, Saturn, Uranus and Neptune, which scientists wanted to investigate.

Once the original mission was accomplished, Voyager 1 used the planets Jupiter and Saturn to perform a gravitational slingshot.

It flew close to each planet, got pulled and accelerated by their massive gravity, and swung around faster than before. And that is how Voyager 1 has achieved its speed.

In the vacuum of space, nothing slows you down.

On Valentine's Day 1990, from beyond Neptune, Voyager 1 took the only portrait of the solar system from the outside ever made by a spacecraft.

In 2012, it became the first human-made object to reach interstellar space.

Its journey is ongoing. In about 40,000 years — long after its plutonium power supply dies out — it will still be flying and pass by the star Gliese 445, which is more than 17 light-years away from Earth.

That's light-years, not light-days. A light-year is the distance light travels in a year. It will take Voyager 1 about 18,000 years to reach the one light-year milestone.

Voyager 1 is destined to wander the Milky Way "perhaps eternally," as NASA puts it.

The spacecraft is the first human-made object ever to be one light-day away from home.

"If you want to say 'hello' to Voyager, you send your message and then you go home and you wait two days before you finally get a response," says Suzanne Dodd, the mission's project manager at NASA's Jet Propulsion Laboratory.

But you do get a response. Even today it sends back data that helps scientists on Earth.

"[It's] still working great, sending back data from a region of space no other human-made craft has ever explored," says Kareem Badaruddin, the mission manager at JPL.

No story about Voyager 1 seems complete without mentioning The Golden Record, a phonographic record, that is part of the spacecraft's cargo.

The spacecraft is carrying other Earthly objects as messages and artefacts from Earth for extraterrestrials. But the Golden Record is what we tend to focus on because it contains soundbites representing languages and cultures from around our planet.

We're told it's very unlikely that any intelligent lifeform will ever receive the cargo or even know how to play The Golden Record.

But if they do get the record and work out how to play it, the first thing they will hear is a controversial voice — not one of Earthly unity, as was the original idea. Those are the words of Kurt Waldheim, a former UN Secretary-General (1972-81) and Austrian President (1986-92), whose concealed wartime past as a German army officer in the Balkans surfaced a decade after Voyager 1's launch. It was alleged Waldheim had participated in Nazi crimes.

GTA 6 Should Help Boost PS5 And Xbox Sales, But Their Astronomical Prices Could Hurt

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Rockstar's Grand Theft Auto 6 launches on November 19, and it's expected to help boost console sales for PS5 and Xbox Series X|S. But the uptick in hardware sales might not be as dramatic as you might think--and there is a good reason why.

According to Circana's Mat Piscatella, there will be "probably" be a boost to PS5 and Xbox Series X|S sales when GTA 6 comes out, but the uptick could be muted due to the recent PS5 and Xbox Series X|S price hikes. Piscatella said anyone who is excited for GTA 6 and needs a new console might think twice when they see how astronomically expensive new consoles have become today.

Historical prices

When GTA 5 launched in 2013, the average price paid for a new PS3 was $252 and the Xbox 360 was $232. Adjusted for inflation, that would be $363 for the PS3 today and $334 for the Xbox 360. Those prices are significantly below the average price of a new PS5 ($646) and Xbox Series X|S ($663) today.

"Not a good thing"

Earlier this year, Take-Two boss Strauss Zelnick, who oversees the company launching GTA 6, said, "The rising cost of hardware is not a good thing." At the same time, Zelnick isn't worried about the impact on GTA 6, in part because he believes the game is so good that people will find a way to pay for a new console.

 "However, a lower price point for hardware would be a good thing because there’d be more hardware in people’s hands," he said. "Probably the most relevant thing is the world continues to move to open systems, and that will continue.”

Nvidia Earnings Could Decide the Next Move for AI Stocks: 3 Things to Watch

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Nvidia earnings scheduled on November 17, with Wall Street expecting approximately $109 billion in quarterly revenue and $2.47 in adjusted EPS.

Data Center revenue is expected to approach $102 billion as demand for Blackwell GPUs and AI infrastructure remains strong.

Investors will focus on forward guidance, profit margins and leadership uncertainty to determine whether Nvidia can restore confidence across AI stocks.

Nvidia earnings could determine whether Wall Street’s AI trade stages a recovery or faces another wave of selling. The chipmaker is preparing to report its fiscal third-quarter 2027 results on November 17, with analysts expecting revenue of nearly $109 billion. However, strong financial results may not be enough to satisfy investors. Concerns surrounding AI spending, rising memory costs and leadership uncertainty have increased the pressure on the company. With Nvidia shares facing renewed volatility, can its latest earnings restore confidence in AI stocks, or is Wall Street preparing for another sell-off?

Nvidia faces exceptionally high expectations heading into its latest earnings report. Wall Street analysts expect the company to generate approximately $108.85 billion in revenue and $2.47 in adjusted earnings per share for fiscal Q3 2027. That would represent revenue growth of roughly 91% from $57.01 billion reported in the same quarter last year.

The company had previously guided for third-quarter revenue of $108 billion, plus or minus 2%, putting its official forecast broadly in line with analyst expectations. The previous quarter provides a strong benchmark. Nvidia reported $96.2 billion in Q2 FY2027 revenue, up 106% year over year, while adjusted earnings reached $2.22 per share. Its Data Center business generated $89 billion, accounting for the overwhelming majority of quarterly revenue.

Nvidia’s Data Center business remains the biggest driver of its valuation, making this segment the most important part of the upcoming earnings report. Analyst estimates put fiscal Q3 Data Center revenue at approximately $101.8 billion. Demand for Blackwell GPUs and expanding AI infrastructure investments from cloud providers are expected to support the increase.

Investors will also examine the company’s Vera Rubin platform, which is expected to become an increasingly important contributor to future revenue. Nvidia has said that Vera Rubin is entering production, while its earlier guidance indicated that the platform could account for around 20% of Data Center revenue in Q3.

The bigger question is whether hyperscalers and AI companies will continue spending aggressively on computing capacity. Microsoft, Amazon, Google and other major technology companies remain important customers in the AI infrastructure cycle.

If Nvidia signals that demand remains strong into fiscal 2028, it could support sentiment across the semiconductor sector. Any indication of slower orders or delayed infrastructure spending, however, could pressure not only NVDA but also AMD, Broadcom and other AI-linked stocks.

Nvidia’s earnings call will face another important test: whether rising costs and business uncertainty are beginning to affect its growth outlook. The company previously guided for a non-GAAP gross margin of approximately 74% in fiscal Q3. However, management also warned that higher memory costs could push margins lower in the following quarter, with a projected range of 71%–72% for Q4 FY2027.

China exposure is another issue investors will monitor. Nvidia’s previous Q3 revenue outlook did not assume any Data Center compute revenue from China, leaving investors to assess how much additional growth could come from other markets.

Leadership uncertainty adds another layer to the earnings event. Recent reports surrounding Jensen Huang’s departure and the company’s leadership transition have increased investor concerns ahead of the results. Interim leadership will need to reassure investors about product execution, customer relationships and the company’s long-term strategy.

The earnings call could therefore matter as much as the financial numbers. Investors will be looking for clear answers on future demand, margins and management’s confidence in the AI growth cycle.

Nvidia stock is approaching a decisive earnings test after losing nearly one-third of its value from its October record high. NVDA stock is trading around $159.92 on November 16, down approximately 32% from the October 6 peak of $243.37. The recent decline has brought the stock close to its 52-week low of $148.80.

The earnings reaction will likely determine which direction NVDA takes next. Strong results, upbeat Data Center guidance and confidence from interim CEO Colette Kress could help buyers regain control. James Bond Fans React To Robert Pattinson's 007 Casting

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Robert Pattinson has spent his career ping-ponging between major franchise fare ("Harry Potter," "Twilight," "The Batman") and weird arthouse fare ("The Lighthouse," "Good Time," "Cosmopolis"). Along the way, he's garnered a pretty broad fanbase, so there are plenty of people celebrating the news that Pattinson will be taking over the role of James Bond.

There were several other finalists to play the next Bond, including Josh O'Connor, Jack Lowden, and Nicholas Hoult, but Pattinson was the clear frontrunner even before he was announced. Early reactions to the news have noted the somewhat absurd flex of landing the role of James Bond just a few years after landing the role of Batman. "This is the greed they talk about in the Bible," wrote one user on Reddit.

Casting such a well-known actor in the role of Bond bucks the trend of 007 casting, with previous revamps traditionally favoring actors who weren't already firmly established. Many people, while not necessarily disliking the choice of Pattinson for the role, have pointed out that Amazon isn't exactly taking a risk. Other Reddit users describe Pattinson as "a very conservative choice" and "very safe casting," with one wryly commenting, "This isn't beating the allegations of 'every movie has the same five actors in it.'" So, while casting a lesser-known actor would surely have attracted some controversy in its own right, some Bond fans feel that the safety of this choice is "disappointing."

And, of course, there are Batman fans who fear that this fresh commitment spells the end of Pattinson's tenure as the Dark Knight. Between production delays on "The Batman II," a recent power shake-up at DC Studios, and now this 007 casting news, the general assumption is that Pattinson's Batman will be hanging up his cape after two movies.

One of the upsides of having such an eccentric career and personality is that Robert Pattinson has generated a great many memes over the years — and now they're being put to good use. One Tumblr user adapted a famous "Twilight" scene to have Pattinson's Edward Cullen saying, "This is the skin of a [license to kill]er," Bella." Others, meanwhile, have joked that we "finally have a Gen Z James Bond," in reference to a Vanity Fair interview wherein Pattinson doggedly insists that he is Gen Z and that the year he was born "doesn't matter."

One meme that became popular even before Pattinson was officially cast imagines the reactions of the other hopefuls auditioning for the role when they see the unstoppable Pattinson walk in after them.

every other actor when robert pattinson shows up to audition pic.twitter.com/40fHsnS2C4

— ivy (@ohhhhhherewegoo) November 16, 2026

Fans are particularly amused by the fact that Pattinson showed up "late" to the audition process (which has been going on for years), sandwiching it in between his commitments to "Dune: Part Three" and "The Batman Part II," and still landed the role. But that kind of effortless swagger only makes him more perfect for the role of James Bond.

Tesla confirms €3B Cybercab factory at Giga Berlin for up to 1M a year

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Tesla is going to build the Cybercab in Germany. The company confirmed today a first €3 billion investment in a new production building at Giga Berlin designed for up to 1 million Cybercabs a year, with production planned for 2029.

That’s a lot of robotaxis for a continent where Tesla can’t operate a single one. Under current EU rules, a driverless vehicle model is capped at 1,500 registrations a year.

Plant manager André Thierig announced the decision on X after Tesla told employees on Monday:

If you ever wondered, what we would be doing at Giga Berlin with all the land that we have… We are expanding! Cybercab production is coming to Giga Berlin! Today we announced a first investment of around € 3b in a new manufacturing building with an anticpated start of… pic.twitter.com/vpcDdxoqlW

Per Tesla’s statement, the €3 billion pays for the new production hall north of the existing plant, plus ancillary buildings and a second wastewater treatment plant. Tesla plans to file the building application before the end of the year, break ground in the first half of 2027, and start production in 2029. The company says it will be “the technologically most advanced Tesla factory in the world.”

It leaked a few hours earlier. Steven Scholtysek, an automation engineer at the plant, posted that Elon Musk personally signed off on the project last week and that the new hall will be bigger than the original factory.

The building isn’t new. Tesla laid out a plan to take Giga Berlin to 1 million vehicles a year in its 2023 permit filings and has been clearing land for it since. What’s new is the product. Until today, Tesla had only said the second hall wouldn’t be another Model Y factory.

Giga Berlin still builds only the Model Y. Tesla has been hiring again at Giga Berlin this year as European demand came back, with a goal of 7,500 cars a week. Even at that pace, the plant would fall short of the 500,000 units a year Tesla promised when it opened in 2022.

Tesla is also adding 4680 battery cell production at the site, with 18 GWh of annual capacity planned.

So the new hall alone is rated for twice what the entire existing plant was supposed to build. And it comes after Musk tied the Berlin expansion to the plant’s works council election in March, warning workers against IG Metall’s influence.

The EU has a type-approval path for fully driverless vehicles, but it’s a “small series” scheme limited to 1,500 registrations per vehicle type per year across the whole EU. In March, the Commission lifted that cap for automated valet parking. Robotaxis are still stuck in the small-series track.

Tesla also has no approval anywhere in Europe for its software to drive without supervision. The Dutch RDW approved the supervised version, now called Tesla Assisted Driving, and nine other countries recognize that approval. It still requires a driver paying attention. The Cybercab has no steering wheel.

Rules can move in three years. The Commission has said it wants large-series approval for other driverless uses, and a UN regulation for automated driving systems is in the works. But as of today, Tesla is committing €3 billion to a vehicle it can’t sell in volume in Europe.

Tesla could export from Berlin, of course. But 1 million Cybercabs a year on top of Giga Texas implies a robotaxi business many times bigger than anything on the road today. Texas, where Cybercab production started in April, had 463 Cybercabs registered as of late October. And Tesla just opened personal Cybercab orders at $29,990 plus a mandatory $299 monthly subscription, usable only inside its Robotaxi geofences.

Tesla can’t even launch its driver-assist system in Europe under its own name. What it’s rolling out there isn’t “Full Self-Driving.” It’s “Tesla Assisted Driving,” a level 2 system that needs a driver watching the road at all times, and it took a long fight with regulators to get it approved in 10 countries.

The Cybercab is a much harder sell. No steering wheel, no pedals, so it has to be certified as a level 4 vehicle with nobody inside to take over. That’s a completely different approval from anything Tesla has gotten in Europe. As of this spring, the EU said no automaker had even used its existing driverless approval path.

And the US program doesn’t inspire confidence. Robotaxi is still confined to geofences, still off highways, and it keeps crashing: Tesla had reported 22 incidents to NHTSA by July, and our analysis of the data earlier this year put it at one crash every 57,000 miles, roughly 9 times worse than the average human driver, with a safety monitor on board. The only reason those crashes don’t get more attention is that they happen at low speed. Of course they do. Tesla keeps the cars on slow city streets. Put the same system on the autobahn and we’d expect more crashes, and worse ones.

This is Musk’s modus operandi for the last few years: bet on autonomy before it’s ready. Tesla removed the Cybercab’s steering wheel assuming the software would be solved by the time it mattered. It wasn’t, and now Tesla is putting €3 billion into a German factory on the same assumption.

Good for the workers in Grünheide, and maybe the hall can build something else if this doesn’t work out. But spending €3 billion to build up to 1 million steering-wheel-less cars a year for a market where Tesla can’t legally run a single one, based on a program that hasn’t left the geofence in Texas, is extremely risky.

Will there be one unsupervised Cybercab carrying paying passengers in Europe when the Grünheide line starts in 2029? We wouldn’t bet on it.

With electricity rates up almost 10% last year and expected to keep climbing, going solar is one of the best ways to protect yourself against rising costs. Judge Sets New Date in Lindsay Clancy Trial

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

A Massachusetts judge announced on Monday that a new trial would begin on March 29 to determine whether Lindsay Clancy murdered her three children in 2023. Clancy was tried this past summer, but in September, the jury announced it was deadlocked and ended the proceeding in a mistrial. Prosecutors have not yet indicated whether they would try Clancy, whose previous trial lasted six weeks and was a cause célèbre, for first-degree murder charges, lesser counts, or at all, according to The New York Times, despite Judge William Sullivan’s announcement.

That said, prosecutors’ agreement to another trial suggests they will pursue first-degree murder charges since, the Times reports, a judge could have ruled on second-degree murder or manslaughter charges without a trial.

The previous trial ended with 11 jurors wanting to acquit Clancy for the deaths of her children — Cora, 5, Dawson, 3, and 8-month-old Callan — and one man, Michael P. Desronvil, insisting she was guilty. Another juror described him as “very arrogant” and claimed he had admitted he had reasonable doubts about her guilt. He disagreed with that claim. “Based on all the physical evidence, key witnesses and what the prosecution presented, I thought it was enough proof that she knew exactly what she was doing and planned,” he said in a September statement. Trending Stories Raye, Rosalía, and Ella Langley Lead 2027 Grammy Nominees Jennifer Lopez Teases New Music on ‘Fallon’: ‘This Does Feel Like a Happy Era’ Cynthia Erivo, Travis Barker Honor Phil Collins With Big Vocals, Big Drums at Rock Hall Ceremony Robert Pattinson Is the Next James Bond

Since the mistrial, a GoFundMe campaign benefiting Clancy’s parents quickly raised more than $1.1 million since their lives were “put on hold.” Clancy’s lawyer, Kevin Reddington, also petitioned President Trump to pardon his client. Reddington, who sees another trial as double jeopardy, has said he feels it’s cruel to put Clancy through another trial.

Clancy remains in custody at a state hospital, according to the Times. Her lawyer had argued that she was experiencing postpartum depression and had hallucinated a voice that told her to strangle her children and kill herself. The case’s next hearing is scheduled for Dec. 30.

Whether you prefer warm or cold sensations, temperature play during a blow job (with your partner’s permission, of course) can really heighten the experience. “Drinking a hot drink like tea or hot cocoa [beforehand] can give your lover a warm sensation once you begin sucking on them,” Lawless says. “For a cold feeling, you can use ice cubes or peppermints in your mouth, and then after sucking on the penis, blow on it to give an extra cooling sensation.”

No one thinks about wearing a condom for a blow job, but practicing safe oral sex is just as important as practicing safe penetrative sex. “Using condoms during fellatio protects against sexually transmitted infections (STIs), offering both partners safety for a more relaxed and enjoyable experience,” Lawless says. “They can also aid in simplifying the cleanup process.” Luckily, there are flavored condoms that can make the experience more pleasant.

“They taste really good, and the lubricant used is PH-balanced, FDA-cleared, and has no spermicide,” sex therapist Rachel Wright, LMFT, previously told Women’s Health. “They’re also made with sustainably-sourced, natural rubber latex, which many flavored condoms are not.”

Ultimately, different blow job techniques work for different people, and it’s perfectly normal to try several techniques before you find a strategy that works for you and your partner. In addition to all the techniques already described, Lawless recommends alternating between “soft and hard” pressure with your tongue to provide varied sensations for the receiver. “You can also lick from the tip of the [penis] head to the testicles and even below the perineum and anus for analingus,” she says. Just remember: Always establish consent before exploring a new area of your partner’s body.

The stakes can feel high when you're going down, but try to release control over whether the receiver ejaculates or how long the BJ takes. “Focusing on the journey of pleasure—both in receiving and giving—is a key piece of satisfying your partner,” Skyler says.

Not to mention, “it reduces performance anxiety for both partners,” adds Lawless, “leading to a more satisfying and intimate encounter.”

One thing that’s easy to forget when giving a blow job is the cleanup phase! Not super fun, necessarily, but still important—especially because, given your outstanding work, there might be something to wipe up.

For this reason, you should always keep a towel nearby, says Lawless. If you want to elevate the experience, consider leaving for a moment and coming back with two washcloths, she adds: “One as a hot towel to erotically wipe your partner down, and another to fluff them dry. It can be a fun way to offer a full-service treatment!”

While the receiver might be basking in a post-blowjob glow, it's important for both partners to make time for aftercare. This is crucial for all intimate encounters, but especially ones that are as vulnerable as oral sex.

“Aftercare is a great way to follow fellatio,” Lawless says. After cleaning up, this might look like fostering emotional intimacy through cuddling, or it might take the more practical form of using mouthwash or brushing your teeth. There's no wrong way to do aftercare so long as “it helps partners nurture each other, take care of each other, and transition from being sexual humans to going back into their everyday lives,” psychologist and sex therapist Kate Balestrieri, PsyD, previously told Women's Health.

Communication can be a crucial part of aftercare—with benefits that last far longer than the average BJ. “Talk about what was great and what you may want to avoid next time to up your blow job game,” says Lawless. “Great lovers always share preferences and feedback to help make sex pleasurable and fun.”

2027 Baseball Hall of Fame ballot released: 8 things to know, including newcomers, trends and a prediction

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

The ballot for the BBWAA portion of the 2027 Baseball Hall of Fame vote was revealed on Monday, featuring 13 holdovers and 14 newcomers.

The full ballot, in alphabetical order, is Bobby Abreu, Jake Arrieta, Jay Bruce, Mark Buehrle, Asdrúbal Cabrera, Starlin Castro, Wade Davis, Brett Gardner, Cole Hamels, Félix Hernández, Torii Hunter, Jon Lester, Andrew Miller, Dustin Pedroia, Andy Pettitte, Buster Posey, Alex Rodriguez, Francisco Rodríguez, Jimmy Rollins, Pablo Sandoval, Ervin Santana, Kyle Seager, Joakim Soria, Chase Utley, Omar Vizquel, David Wright and Ryan Zimmerman. Newcomers to the ballot are in bold.

As a reminder, players must receive votes on at least 75% of the ballots cast to be inducted into the Hall of Fame with the 2027 class. Those who get at least 5% of the vote can remain on the ballot the following year, and so on, for a maximum of 10 voting cycles. Players who are on the ballot for 10 years without getting 75% of the vote, in addition to players not getting 5% of the vote, fall off the ballot in ensuing years, though that doesn't mean the doors to the Hall are permanently shut.

The results will be announced on Jan. 19, with the induction set for Cooperstown over the summer.

Here's everything else you need to know about the Baseball Hall of Fame this offseason.

As alluded to earlier, there are two different votes each offseason. The BBWAA vote is for former players who have been retired from Major League Baseball for at least five years. It's the most common method in mainstream Hall of Fame ballot discussions. When you hear stuff like "first-ballot Hall of Famer," it means via the BBWAA vote.

Players who don't make it via the BBWAA vote can get extra chances via committees. That's how, for example, Jeff Kent made it last year. Formerly called the Veterans Committee, the Hall of Fame now uses Era Committees, which are divided into two categories: contemporary baseball (1980 to present) and classic (prior to 1980). The contemporary ballot is then split into two, one for players and one for non-players like umpires, executives, pioneers and managers.

Sure enough, this time around, it is a non-player Contemporary Baseball Committee, which looks at "managers, umpires and executives whose most significant career impact was realized since 1980."

We'll dive further into this group as we get closer to the Dec. 6 vote.

Giants legend Buster Posey joins the ballot, and he's one of those cases where I don't think a bunch of people need to delve deeply into his numbers. More than statistics, sometimes we get "feel" guys, as in, you just know he's a Hall of Famer. Posey qualifies there, I would think, for 75% of the electorate. We'll obviously do a deeper dive in the coming weeks, but even without the longevity component (Posey only played in parts of 12 MLB seasons), I think he's in.

As for the rest of the first-timers, the best case would be Jon Lester. Given the way a lot of similar starting pitchers have been treated in voting in recent years, my hunch is he'll be able to hang around on the ballot, but he has a major uphill battle in order to get in.

Then you're looking at Brett Gardner, Ryan Zimmerman, Kyle Seager, the short peak of Jake Arrieta and relief work of Andrew Miller. Good players worth mentioning, of course, but they won't sniff the Hall and it seems likely all behind Posey and Lester are one-and-dones on this ballot, as they'll fall short of 5% of the vote.

This is the sixth year for Alex Rodriguez on the ballot, which means it's the sixth time that I point out that we don't really need much time to go over his case. His numbers say he's one of the 10 best position players of all time, but his ties to PEDs are keeping him out to this point.

He did rise from 37.1% of the vote in 2025 to 40% last time, a new high-water mark. The progress is too modest to believe it's trending toward him getting in, but the number this year is certainly worth watching. If he somehow makes a leap to the 50% range, a deeper conversation might be in order.

While some voters don't mind sending in blank ballots to prove some sort of nebulous point, most impose limits on themselves. Whether it's the actual rule of 10 or some voters saying they'll only vote, for example, for a maximum of three players on any given ballot, relatively weak ballots can leave openings for the best players to show huge gains. With Carlos Beltràn and Andruw Jones getting in last year and only one strong first-year candidate, perhaps Chase Utley can benefit.

Utley is only in his fourth year on the ballot, but he's already made significant gains:

He only needs to add 15.9 points after jumping nearly 20 last voting cycle. The situation is ripe for Utley to either get all the way in or get close enough to where he probably makes the Hall of Fame in 2028.

Some of us are pushing to lower the Hall of Fame standard on modern starting pitchers, given the decreases in workload and how it affects their career numbers. We might be making some headway, but it's still tough sledding. Here are the holdover starting pitchers and their standing:

The bar for relievers to make the Hall of Fame is justly pretty high. We recently saw Billy Wagner make a late move and make it in his last try on the BBWAA ballot. Francisco Rodríguez has had four chances on the ballot and gotten between 7.8% and 11.8% of the vote thus far.

K-Rod ranks sixth all time in saves and has good rate stats. He's worth tracking as it relates to three active relievers. Kenley Jansen is third in saves with a similar number of innings pitched to K-Rod. Craig Kimbrel is fifth and has more than 100 fewer innings pitched. Aroldis Chapman is ninth and in the ballpark of Kimbrel in workload to this point.

If K-Rod continues to have trouble making headway toward induction, does that mean Kimbrel and Chapman will have trouble as well? What about Jansen?

Who else is back on the ballot? A quick look:

Posey is a lock. If the committee era chooses Bochy, Baker and/or Sabean, that would be some nice San Francisco flavor, too, right? I'll predict that committee ballot at a later date, though.

I'll also say Utley's good roll here continues with a relatively weak ballot (at least when you factor in what the PED stain has done to some guys).

On the player side, my 2027 Hall of Fame class prediction is Buster Posey and Chase Utley.

Nvidia and IBM’s 13,000x Quantum Speedup Raises New ‘Q-Day’ Questions for Bitcoin

📅 2026-11-16  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Nvidia, IBM and Fermilab announced new tools for quantum error correction on Nov. 16, and the news comes amid a debate in the crypto industry about "Q-Day," the moment when a quantum computer will be able to break Bitcoin's cryptography. As of Monday, the price of Bitcoin stands at $64,580.

Nvidia released a new decoding algorithm, TCBP-JOBS, on Nov. 16. According to the company, the algorithm delivers up to 13,000x faster decoding compared with existing leading algorithms, and it cuts the computation time for researching new quantum computing designs from 12 weeks to 45 minutes.

Nvidia said that the algorithm helps researchers find designs that use physical qubits more efficiently, "shortening the path to practical fault-tolerant quantum computing."

Nvidia and IBM also announced plans for a modular quantum computing codesign platform with Fermilab. IBM and Nvidia are placing Nvidia DGX B200 systems next to IBM quantum computers, including those powered by IBM Quantum Nighthawk, and researchers will use the platform to develop and test error-correction codes.

"Making quantum computers useful for science requires advances in error correction, applications and the systems that bring quantum processors and accelerated computing together," said Krysta Svore, vice president of applied research for quantum computing at Nvidia.

A 2022 study by researchers from the University of Sussex estimated that breaking Bitcoin's encryption within a day would require around 13 million physical qubits.

IBM Quantum Nighthawk, which IBM calls its fastest quantum processor, has 120 qubits. According to Nvidia, errors in quantum processors "limit the complexity and duration of the computations they can perform."

In 2024, Bitcoin developer Hunter Beast proposed BIP-360, a new output type that would protect coins from quantum attacks. In July 2025, Casa co-founder Jameson Lopp also suggested a plan to phase out legacy signatures and move coins to quantum-resistant addresses.

Early coins mined by Satoshi Nakamoto sit on addresses with public keys that are already visible on the blockchain.

In November 2024, NIST proposed to stop allowing vulnerable public-key algorithms, including elliptic curve cryptography, after 2035.

Trump leaves door open to Kim Jong Un joining high-stakes talks with Xi, Putin

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

President Donald Trump is set to meet with Chinese President Xi Jinping and Russian President Vladimir Putin during his Asia trip, and he left open the possibility that North Korean leader Kim Jong Un could join the high-stakes talks.

President Donald Trump offered a brief, unexpected response about North Korea during a White House gaggle Monday, adding a new wrinkle to his closely watched trip to Asia this week.

Trump suggested North Korean leader Kim Jong Un could make an appearance during his China trip, telling reporters, "We might have Kim Jong Un. He may come, I don’t know. That’s up to President Xi."

The comment came in response to a reporter's question about the upcoming high-stakes meetings between Trump, Chinese President Xi Jinping, and Russian President Vladimir Putin at the sidelines of the Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation (APEC) Leaders' Meeting in Shenzhen, China, and whether the trio planned to discuss North Korean nuclear capabilities.

PRESIDENT TRUMP TO HOLD HISTORIC TRILATERAL SUMMIT WITH XI AND PUTIN

President Donald Trump is expected to meet with Chinese President Xi Jinping and Russian President Vladimir Putin Thursday. (Reuters)

Trump and Kim last met in 2019, when their Hanoi summit ended without an agreement on North Korea’s nuclear program. Nonetheless, Pyongyang has since expanded its nuclear and missile capabilities, while efforts to restart talks have stalled. In August, Trump said he expected to meet Kim "later this year," but North Korea pushed back, with Kim Yo Jong saying the leaders’ relationship remained "excellent" while accusing Washington of maintaining hostile policies toward Pyongyang, Fox News Digital reported at the time.

Meanwhile, a U.S. official said last week, according to ABC News, that there were no plans for North Korea engagement during Trump’s Asia trip, and experts say the country is unlikely to feature prominently in the trilateral talks.

South Korean news outlet Yonhap News Agency reported Monday that South Korea’s Foreign Ministry said it hoped high-level talks on the sidelines of the APEC summit would contribute to peace and stability on the Korean Peninsula. South Korean Foreign Minister Cho Hyun reportedly raised the possibility of renewed U.S.-North Korea dialogue at the summit with his Chinese counterpart, Wang Yi, in Beijing on Friday, stressing that South Korea has vital interests in Korean Peninsula issues and hopes that its perspective is taken into account.

NORTH KOREA MISSILE COULD PUT GUAM IN RANGE AS KIM REBUFFS TRUMP OUTREACH: EXPERT

President Donald Trump meets with North Korean leader Kim Jong Un at the North Korean side of the border at the village of Panmunjom in Demilitarized Zone, on June 30, 2019. (Susan Walsh/AP)

The South Korean news agency also reported Monday that Andrew Yeo, a senior fellow at the U.S.-based Brookings Institution, said North Korea’s nuclear program is unlikely to be discussed at the summit, citing differences between Washington and Moscow over Pyongyang’s nuclear ambitions. The report also highlighted comments from Korea Economic Institute senior fellow Ellen Kim, who reportedly suggested the leaders could briefly discuss North Korea behind closed doors, but nuclear arms control and the war in Iran are likely to take priority. Ellen Kim added that Xi or Putin could relay a message from Trump to Kim Jong Un, though they may be reluctant to support U.S.-North Korea negotiations for fear of losing influence over Pyongyang.

The trilateral meeting is scheduled for 2:30 p.m. Thursday local time at Shenzhen Kylin Villa, according to Trump’s public schedule.

White House spokeswoman Olivia Wales said Friday that Trump planned to discuss nuclear arms reduction with Xi and Putin, while a senior U.S. official said Iran would also be on the agenda. The Associated Press reported Monday that Trump plans to raise Russia’s war in Ukraine at the meeting as well.

Russian President Vladimir Putin greets Chinese President Xi Jinping as Foreign Minister Sergei Lavrov, second from right, and First Deputy Prime Minister Igor Shuvalov, right, look on during their meeting in the Grand Kremlin Palace in Moscow on May 8, 2015. (Sasha Mordovets/Getty Images)

Asked Monday what it would take for Putin to back down from the war in Ukraine, Trump said he thinks Putin "is backing down a little bit."

"I think he's backing down a little bit. I think he could be tougher," Trump told reporters Monday outside the White House. "I think it could be a lot tougher, frankly."

Fox News Digital has reached out to the White House for additional comment.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

What do you mean bad actors I wasn’t doing that much bad things pic.twitter.com/swHfeOLTuVNovember 16, 2026

Bitly, the URL shortener website, got in hot water this week as users (or 'bad actors' as the company calls them) found out they could hijack old broken links and put whatever they like in their place. As you can imagine, chaos ensued.

Many of the links have since been fixed, but it seems like the McDonald's Twitter account once accidentally said the Swedish McDonald's team are responsible for 9/11,, and KFC linked to the Mewgenics wiki page for Steven, an NPC from the game. McDonald's was caught multiple times in this hijacking, with the Canadian X account hijacked to link the We Are Charlie Kirk song.

Though it's hard to verify now, it also seems like the Windows X page accidentally linked to the "r/FuckMicrosoft" subreddit too.

Big brands weren't the only ones caught in the mass hijacking. President Donald Trump seemingly tweeted a lot of Bitly links back in 2011, which were hijacked to instead link to a 'Trollface' meme song, a picture of a soggy cat titled "Soggy cat", and a link to the Epstein files Wikipedia page.

images for archival purposes pic.twitter.com/fRRr8LMGeENovember 15, 2026

A day after this exploit started to make its way around X, Bitly shared that it had identified the issue and said, "We have suspended the accounts involved and are disabling the affected links. Our Trust and Safety team is working to address the issue and strengthen protections against similar misuse in the future."

It seems like the links in question were broken before being hijacked, and users simply managed to redirect those links to whatever site they wanted. However, in Bitly's documentation, it says the "back-halves used on these links cannot be re-used — whether they're automatically-generated or custom back-halves." So it's not entirely clear how these users manage to use links when Bitly says they supposedly cannot be reused.

Earlier today, we identified an issue that allowed bad actors to repurpose certain old, inactive links to redirect people to content unrelated to the original posts. We have suspended the accounts involved and are disabling the affected links. Our Trust and Safety team is working…November 15, 2026

In the grand scheme, brands linking to various songs and Wikipedia articles are relatively harmless, but the ability to hijack links does pose a problem: what if hackers could do the same with a link to something containing malware? As such, it's probably a good reminder to Bitly and any other URL shortener websites to double-check that users can't reuse old broken links, as who knows who could have possibly made them first.

Anthony Boyle Plays the Ultimate Boy Wonder Tech Bro in The Altruists

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Before Anthony Boyle and I begin our interview, we’ve already covered a fair amount of ground. Serendipitously, we’ve ridden up alongside each other on our bicycles. Boyle is still acquainting himself with the city’s two-wheeled possibilities; he’s a recent but enthusiastic convert. “It opens up the world!” he declares.

Our first stop is a tiny gallery in east London, where an exhibition of contemporary Irish art is on display. Boyle, a Belfast transplant, has lived in London off and on for the past few years. “Is that the Big Fish? It’s surely the Big Fish,” Boyle asks of one painting, invoking the giant salmon sculpture that sits on the waterfront in Belfast. The actor is wearing jeans, low-profile sneakers, and a blue T-shirt with a horse printed on the back, a recent impulse purchase—it reminds him of childhood summer vacations spent riding in Donegal.

Recently, Boyle, 32, has established himself with his shrewd portrayal of IRA commander Brendan “The Dark” Hughes in the TV series Say Nothing and his assured turn as the flamboyant Arthur Guinness in Steven Knight’s House of Guinness. Earlier this year, the film I See Buildings Fall Like Lightning—in which Boyle plays a college-educated food-delivery cyclist—premiered to impassioned reviews at Cannes. “The film feels like a socialist war cry,” Boyle says, describing its portrayal of working-class communities in modern Britain.

And this November, he took another exhilarating left turn, starring opposite Julia Garner in Netflix’s The Altruists, an eight-part series about the spectacular collapse of the cryptocurrency exchange FTX—and one of a handful of new films and TV series that spin our recent techno-history into entertainment. Boyle plays the company’s disgraced founder, Sam Bankman-Fried, whose dizzying ascent and precipitous downfall have made him a hubristic emblem of the crypto age. The show casts Bankman-Fried and his on-again, off-again girlfriend and colleague Caroline Ellison (Garner) as hyperintelligent idealists, at once fiercely competitive and committed to “effective altruism”—a philosophical movement that seeks to optimize charitable giving.

The awkward physicality, unruly hair, and peculiar vocal inflections that Boyle brought to the role are a world away from the steely conviction of an IRA soldier or the patrician swagger of a Guinness. For his first meeting with The Altruists’ creators, he went to the budget retailer Primark, bought a black sweater, smeared it with Cup Noodle stains, and mussed his hair. He logged onto Zoom in character, improvising that he had Mark Zuckerberg waiting on another call. His childhood friend and Say Nothing costar Lola Petticrew, with whom he rehearsed, offered invaluable feedback. “When you do that voice, I want to throw a cup of tea over you,” Boyle remembers Petticrew saying. He was delighted. “There’s an inner conflict in him that makes his performances unpredictable,” says Garner. “Whatever he’s thinking or feeling, you can see it in his eyes. You can’t teach someone to have those eyes.”

Once he was offered the role, Boyle watched footage of Bankman-Fried, fascinated by an awkwardness at odds with his extraordinary influence. He even corresponded with Bankman-Fried in prison: “This is the smartest person I’ve ever been in contact with,” he says of their exchange. “People don’t think they’re doing evil,” Boyle says, reflecting on Bankman-Fried’s motivations. “They believe they’re doing things for the greater good.”

PARALLEL LINES Anthony Boyle wears a Dries Van Noten sweater, JW Anderson jeans, and Paul Smith belt.

As a boy in West Belfast, Boyle trained at the Royal Welsh College of Music & Drama before his onstage breakout at age 21 as Scorpius Malfoy in Harry Potter and the Cursed Child, which earned him an Olivier Award. “Before that, my longest play was a five-day run at a community center,” he says. In addition to his striking Say Nothing and House of Guinness performances, Boyle’s career has encompassed roles in the World War II drama Masters of the Air and the John Wilkes Booth–inspired series Manhunt. “I met with a bunch of cowboys,” he says of that role, “it took me a while to lose the habit of picking up random things, chewing, spitting them out.” He delights in the contrasts in his work: “I’m always trying to do what I haven’t done,” he says. As we walk through the park, a few men practicing martial arts catch Boyle’s attention, and he acquires a pair of nunchucks, giving them a whirl with enough confidence that I can’t discern whether this is a genuine skill or a gloriously committed bluff. “Pretty good, right?” he asks.

Since The Altruists finished shooting last November, Boyle completed the forthcoming dark comedy The Worst (with Keira Knightley and Alicia Vikander) and has been filming Charmer, a new series in which he plays a professional seducer hired to test the fidelity of his wealthy clients’ partners. Next comes a second season of House of Guinness, with just two days off between filming. “I need to find little bits of joy and craic where I can,” he says.

One of those joys is returning home. Boyle has lived in London and New York and recently moved back to Belfast, although his schedule means he seldom spends much time there. Recently at a McDonald’s with his cousin, Boyle’s order was supersized without any prompting. “Anything for The Dark,” the server said—a reference to his Say Nothing role, and, Boyle reckons, just about the coolest thing that could have happened on his home turf. He’s been honored by his hometown in additional ways: Belfast artist Colin Davidson recently painted his portrait and unveiled it at the city’s Lyric Theatre. Davidson offered him some sage advice while the actor was sitting for him: “He said, ‘Never soften your edges, Anthony.’ ”

“Coming from where we come from, this career seemed a world away, but seeing him chase it made it feel more tangible for me,” says Petticrew. Working together on Say Nothing, Petticrew adds, was meaningful beyond the acting: “To reflect the place we both love so deeply and tell its story was probably one of the most important things we’ll ever do.”

An upcoming project will bring Boyle even nearer to his origins. In a four-part adaptation of Michael Magee’s acclaimed 2023 novel Close to Home, he plays Sean, a young man returning to a Belfast reckoning with the aftermath of the sectarian violence of the Troubles. Boyle attended the same school as Magee a few years apart, and the novel depicts a world Boyle recognizes—down to the hardscrabble streets and poorly paid nightclub jobs. “I felt like someone was reaching out of the book and grabbing me,” he says. “It articulated thoughts and experiences I thought only I had.” Boyle urged the book on everyone around him. “I bought a hundred copies,” he says—one Christmas, he distributed 10 among his family. “None of them opened it.” Television, he realized, might be a way to get the story into their lives.

Playing Sean demanded a new methodology from Boyle: “It was almost coming back to myself in a way and not performing,” he says. Boyle, who is also executive producing the series, championed local talent and developed it closely with Magee, running scenes with him until the dialogue felt lived-in. “I wanted to make Close to Home for people in the areas I grew up in.”

We’re wrapping up our walk when he tells me he is meeting his father to help his younger sister move apartments. She recently arrived in London for college and was temporarily living in his house with a friend while looking for a place of her own. Boyle would leave for work before dawn and return late to find them blasting Sinéad O’Connor and dancing in the kitchen. “It was infectious,” he says. We’re now in front of Hackney City Farm, a cloistered urban oasis with livestock and gardens. His new T-shirt has reminded him how much he misses riding, and he wants to know where in London he can get on a horse. When I mention that the farm has donkeys, he’s thrilled: “I love a donkey.”

A week later, I run into Boyle again, shouting to me as I bike past. This time he’s accompanied by his older sibling and their partner. He’s showing them around before heading to New York for The Altruists press tour. Swaggering along the road, he is just as buoyant as before, taking himself off on another adventurous detour.

In this story: Grooming, Liz Taw; tailor, Nafisa Tosh.

The lost years of Lamar Jackson -- and the greatness still within reach

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

Some years, Lamar Jackson's thumb hits another player and is fine. Some years, his ankle just gets hit rather than rolled up on. And those years, he has been -- and will be -- a player who can win an MVP and perhaps a Super Bowl.

Jackson's latest injury was almost too cruel to believe. After missing the previous three and a half games with a high ankle sprain, the two-time MVP was finally back. So, too, was the Ravens' hope. Jackson had looked phenomenal -- MVP-level phenomenal -- in new offensive coordinator Declan Doyle's system. Not only was he back, but he was back in a big spot: against fellow MVP Josh Allen in a crucial matchup vs. Buffalo on the first day of November, with Baltimore trying to end a two-game losing streak.

And then, on the Ravens' first play, Jackson hit his thumb on Greg Rousseau's arm. Jackson looked down at his injured right thumb, trotted toward the sideline and then took a knee.

"Oh boy," NFL on CBS analyst Ross Tucker said.

Medical personnel surrounded Jackson, draped a towel over his hand and brought him down the tunnel, off the field.

What more was there to say? Lamar Jackson, one of the defining players not just of the era but of NFL history, was hurt. Again. And it was serious. Again.

Jackson officially landed on IR with a thumb injury that will require surgery. It is "potentially" season-ending, and with the Ravens 4-5 and losers of four straight, it's looking like a lost season in Baltimore. This is the fourth time in the last six years the 29-year-old has missed at least four games. It has robbed the Ravens of their Super Bowl aspirations. It has robbed the NFL of a superstar. And it has robbed Jackson, a Heisman Trophy winner and twice an NFL MVP, of even higher levels of all-time greatness.

On the positive side, the thumb injury is the first ailment to land Jackson on IR. He has never had an ACL tear, an Achilles tear, etc. On the negative side, Jackson has racked up so many injuries of so many different kinds that it seems like something is constantly getting in the way, whether it's nagging at him or keeping him out of games. He even said the ankle pain entering the Bills game last week was merely "bearable," not gone.

Since the start of last season alone, Jackson has had hamstring, ankle, toe, knee, back, and thumb ailments. Not all of those kept him out, but they add up. He admitted he came back from last year's hamstring injury too early, impacting his play all season.

And yes, for what it's worth, it's bad luck. If Jackson's hand hits Rousseau at any other angle, he might not hurt his thumb enough to need surgery. If a diving Jermaine Johnson II doesn't awkwardly trap Jackson's ankle to the turf in Week 3, Jackson might not miss the next three games. Josh Allen and Patrick Mahomes have both had hundreds of similar plays and, with the exception of Mahomes' torn ACL last year, avoided injury for the most part. Sometimes, it's simply human anatomy, something Jackson cannot change.

For as prolific a runner as Jackson is, he does an excellent job avoiding hits on those plays. None of his injuries have been on runs. It has just been a constant parade of misfortune. Unfortunately for Jackson and the Ravens, the NFL rolls on, whether you're lucky or not.

Perhaps time and injuries have taken their toll and blocked our collective memory from how incredible Jackson has been. That would be truly unfortunate.

In 2019, Jackson's second year of the league, he threw 36 touchdown passes and ran for 1,206 yards. In NFL history, there are ...

In fact, only one other player ever has posted 1,200 rushing yards and merely double-digit passing touchdowns in a season: Spec Sanders of the New York Yankees (football version) in the All-America Football Conference in 1947. But he only threw 14 touchdowns. Jackson did things modern football has never known.

Jackson became the youngest quarterback to win MVP and joined Tom Brady as the only unanimous MVPs in NFL history. There are many ways to measure individual season greatness, but Pro Football Reference's "Approximate Value" is as good as any. Jackson had an approximate value of 25 in 2019, tied for the second-best season on record; LaDanian Tomlinson's 2006 season, in which he scored an NFL-record 31 touchdowns, is the only one better.

Jackson was healthy in 2020 and had another strong season, including a second straight 1,000-yard rushing effort. He has as many 1,000-yard rushing seasons as all other quarterbacks in NFL history combined (Michael Vick and Justin Fields both have one).

Then the injuries started. In 2021, Jackson was on pace for another 1,000-yard rushing season, and the Ravens led the AFC North when Browns linebacker Jeremiah Owusu-Koramoah dove at Jackson's ankles after Jackson released a pass. Jackson got carted off and didn't play in any of the final four games. The Ravens lost all four and missed the playoffs.

The next year, Jackson sprained his PCL when his left knee got driven into the ground on a sack by the Broncos' Jonathan Cooper. He ended up missing six weeks -- the final five of the regular season and a wild card game, which Baltimore lost.

This was a strange one. John Harbaugh initially wondered if Jackson could be back the next week and added "If not, it'll be sometime after that shortly." Then, Ravens officials were confident Jackson would return for the playoffs, per ESPN, leaving Jackson to essentially rule himself out -- and give his own medical update -- via social media.

In 2023, though, Jackson was healthy and marvelous once again. Under new offensive coordinator Todd Monken, Jackson set career highs in completion percentage (67.2%), passing yards (3,678) and yards per attempt (8.0). He was still excellent on the ground with a team-high 821 yards. He won MVP and was one vote short of doing so unanimously.

He was somehow even better in 2024, with 4,172 passing yards and 8.8 yards per attempt. He threw 41 touchdowns and just four interceptions. He ran for 915 yards. He had already reached unprecedented levels. Now, he was reaching new ones, as the first player with:

His 10.25 touchdown-to-interception ratio was also the best ever in any 30-touchdown season.

Somehow Jackson didn't win MVP. He was a first-team All-Pro, but MVP went to Allen, an extreme rarity. Jackson would have been the youngest three-time MVP in NFL history. That's how good the start was.

From 2019 to 2024, Jackson ranked third in passer rating and touchdown-to-interception ratio and fourth in yards per attempt. He was also fourth in touchdown passes despite missing significant time in two seasons. He was also eighth in rushing yards and first in yards per carry.

He was the Shohei Ohtani of football. No one can be this good at two different aspects, right? Wrong. Jackson was truly exceptional, and he seemed to be getting better.

He had nine touchdowns and zero interceptions on an absurd 9.6 yards per attempt through three weeks of 2025. Then came the hamstring injury, which started an avalanche of injuries. The hits haven't stopped coming since.

If two things stand out as Jackson's career shortcomings, it's injuries and playoff performance. The two are somewhat intertwined. In 2021, Baltimore missed the playoffs. In 2022, Jackson missed the playoffs. Last year, his injuries derailed the Ravens, but they somehow got a Week 18 win-and-in game against the Steelers. Tyler Loop missed a game-winning field goal attempt. It's looking like he will not add to his postseason ledger this season, either.

Had Jackson been healthy in even two of those four lost seasons, is he a Super Bowl champ? Or does he at least have more postseason wins?

Jackson is one of nine quarterbacks to win multiple MVP awards since the 1970 AFL-NFL merger. Of those nine, Jackson has the second-fewest playoff wins (two) before his 30th birthday, only ahead of the famously late-blooming Steve Young.

Wins, it is said, are not a quarterback stat, and that can be true. Unfortunately for Jackson and the Ravens, both his play and his team's have declined in the postseason. The Ravens are 3-6 in the playoffs since drafting Jackson in 2018 (3-5 with Jackson as the starter).

Jackson lost in his playoff debut in 2018, but the real disappointment came after his 2019 NFL season. With the Ravens as the AFC No. 1 seed, he had 365 yards and a touchdown passing and 143 yards rushing against the Titans, but he also had two interceptions,  lost a fumble, and failed on two fourth-and-short runs.

The next year, Jackson earned his first playoff win, getting a measure of revenge on the Titans in the wild card round. But one week later, Baltimore went quietly in a 17-3 loss to Buffalo, most remembered for Jackson throwing a terrible end-zone interception that Taron Johnson returned 101 yards for a touchdown.

The INT that's tied for the longest pick-six in playoff history?This 101-yard return from Taron Johnson in the 2020 Divisional Round 🔥📺: #KCvsBUF — Sunday 6:30pm ET on CBS📱: Stream on #NFLPlus pic.twitter.com/Z9SsFryOEL

Jackson suffered a concussion on the final play of the third quarter and did not return.

His next playoff game came after his 2023 MVP campaign. He was excellent in a win over the Texans, but in the AFC Championship, he lost a fumble and had another awful end-zone interception, this time in the fourth quarter. The Chiefs won 17-10, and Jackson's two turnovers loomed large.

In 2024, the Ravens, supercharged by the Derrick Henry addition, were on the precipice of a return to the AFC Championship Game before falling to the Bills, 27-25. Jackson again had turnover issues -- a bad interception and an even worse fumble, the latter of which led the a Buffalo touchdown -- but that game is better remembered for a Mark Andrews fumble and, later, drop on the potential game-tying two-point attempt.

There have been tons of moments where supporting cast members have let Jackson down in crucial moments. Andrews' nightmare two seasons ago and Zay Flowers' fumble at the 1-yard line against the Chiefs certainly come to mind. There's a lack of game-changing plays from the defense: two turnovers forced in Jackson's seven postseason starts, and none in the past two playoff trips, when hopes were the highest. But it's undeniable that Jackson has been very poor in the playoffs, too.

The final stat -- turnovers -- is truly staggering ... and costly. Jackson's turnovers have cost the Ravens an estimated 6.86 points per game in the playoffs, basically a touchdown. The only quarterbacks with a higher rate out of the 26-player sample are Drake Maye, Justin Herbert, and Trevor Lawrence.

Unless there's a major turnaround, this stands to be the Jackson's fourth postseason-less season in the last six years. He can't fix a glaring hole in his resume if he can't even get there.

The quick answer is "yes." It might just look a little different.

The first order of business, assuming this season is over for all intents and purposes, will be Jackson's contract. Former NFL agent Joel Corry dove into that recently. Of note ...

Baltimore should absolutely want to keep Jackson, even with the injury history. Through three games this year, he led the league in yards per attempt and was on pace for his highest missed-tackle rate and highest yards per rush of his career, all while not running as much as earlier in his career.

They have also been truly unlucky with injuries. He was healthy in 2023 and 2024. Last year's hamstring and this year's ankle were unfortunate, but the thumb was a freak injury. Perhaps we should expect him to miss a few games every year from now on, but there's also a reasonable chance he doesn't.

Jackson will be 30 next year, but 30 is not ancient. John Elway won his first Super Bowl at 37. Matthew Stafford got his first at 34. Drew Brees was 31. Peyton Manning was 30.

Josh Allen is 30 and, like Jackson, hasn't been to a Super Bowl. Joe Burrow is about a month older than Jackson and hasn't won one.

A common budding misconception is that Jackson can't be a star as he gets older and he runs less and/or less effectively. But Jackson has proved to be a superb pocket quarterback. His development as a passer is as impressive as it gets. Since 2023, Jackson has the third-highest yards per attempt in the NFL on throws inside the pocket and released within three seconds -- aka without much improvisation going on.

Jackson may never reach the heights he was on the path to after being on the precipice of becoming the youngest three-time MVP ever in 2024. That's the sad truth about a player playing hurt and/or missing significant chunks of three years of his prime. Would he have another MVP? Two? A Super Bowl ring? We'll never know, and that's tough to swallow.

But there's a version of Jackson -- a little luckier, a little better in the playoffs, a lot better of a passer than he was his younger years -- that can still be a superstar for many years and get him back on a path that ends with him as an all-time great.

‘The only question is if it’s a soft recession or something more dramatic’: Gary Gensler warns the AI bubble is likely to burst and result in a recession before November 2028

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join the Tom's Guide Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.
