The Former Securities and Exchange Commission Chair makes a scary prediction

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

There seems to be a slew of warning signs pointing to the inevitability of the AI bubble bursting a lot sooner than most people think.

The controversial exit of Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang (who happens to be recognized as the "high priest" of the global AI buildout) and the stock market volatility tied to companies that have made huge investments in AI are two of the bigger signs pointing to the AI bubble bursting. Plus, there have been plenty of major personalities in the tech and business worlds who have given their thoughts on it becoming a reality at some point.

One of those individuals is the Former Securities and Exchange Commission Chair, Gary Gensler, who sat down with CNN to give his own take on the possibility of the AI burst event coming to fruition.

Here’s what he had to say and why his prediction places it near the next presidential election.

Gensler, who ran the Securities and Exchange Commission from 2021 until 2025, told CNN that today’s AI infrastructure buildout has already crossed into bubble territory and will likely lead into a recession before the next presidential election. "It's already a bubble," the former Wall Street regulator stated. "The only question is if it's a soft recession or something more dramatic."

On the topic of all the big spending dedicated to strengthening the current AI infrastructure (which is said to have reached $800 billion a year by some estimates), Gensler pointed to past trends where major investments in technology eventually stagnate.

“History tells us that when you have a new technology and a significant investment ... you come to a point where it plateaus. It retrenches,” Gensler noted. “The only question is if it’s a soft recession or something more dramatic.” The previous instances he cited as big tech investments that eventually met steep declines included railroads, 1920s electricity and the 1990s dot-com boom.

Sign up to the Tom's AI Guide weekly newsletter summing up all the biggest AI news you need to know. Plus, analysis from our AI editors and tips on how to use the latest AI tools!

As for when he sees the AI bubble bursting, he points to it going down sometime before the next presidential election. "I think we're going to have this repricing and a softening of the economy that happens before November 2028," he went on to say. "I can't say exactly when and how stormy, but I would be battening down some hatches."

Gensler is among the many other voices in the tech and investment industries who have given their thoughts on the possibilities of the AI bubble bursting and the resulting fallout. Gary Marcus, the former head of AI for Uber, argued to the Financial Times that today’s AI boom is a massive financial bubble driven by unsustainable economics and technological dead ends:

“If there is a deflation of the AI bubble, the optimists say that the new infrastructure will remain even if the companies do not — just as railways survived the 19th-century railway bust. However, this fails to reckon with the reality of depreciation (few pieces of silicon hold their value for very long because better chips inevitably come along) and the possibility that LLMs could be displaced by more efficient models less dependent on massive numbers of expensive AI chips. In placing massive hyperscaling bets, investors are setting lavish expectations about future earnings. But LLMs are not likely to replicate the near monopolies that have made the market power of current tech giants hard to assail. A better analogy for them might be airlines, which are hobbled by small margins, intense competition, high expenses and dependence on hardware created by outside vendors.” – @garymarcus in @FinancialTimesJune 26, 2026

The Canadian mining financier Frank Giustra was quite frank with his AI bubble burst prediction while speaking at this year’s New Orleans Investment Conference. “There will be a reckoning, and some of these companies will get destroyed," he said. During that same conversation, investor Grant Williams compared the disconnect between recognizing the stock market dangers attached to AI investments and taking proper precautions to a natural disaster.

"If we told you a hurricane was heading to New Orleans, everybody would take precautions," he explained. "The interesting thing about stock market bubbles is people don't really think about what can go wrong. They're still thinking about making money."

While many voices are speaking on the very real possibility of an AI bubble burst, there are business bigwigs who have made it clear that everyone shouldn’t be all that worried about it. During a sit-down interview with CNBC, Amazon founder Jeff Bezos tried to ease everyone’s fears about such a financially devastating event coming to pass. “Even if it does turn out to be a bubble, you shouldn’t worry about it because the bubble is driving investment and a lot of the investment is going to turn out to be very healthy,” he proclaimed.

Bret Taylor, the chairman of OpenAI and CEO of Sierra, spoke to The Verge and flat-out acknowledged that an AI bubble exists while still noting that it's totally fine. And while speaking to CNBC at the 2026 World Economic Forum in Davos, Switzerland, his AI optimism pointed to a scenario where the market will correct itself as weaker industry players and overfunded ventures will fall by the wayside.

“When everyone knows that AI is going to have a huge impact on the economy across a huge range of industries and workflows, money is plentiful,” Taylor noted. “I think over the next few years, you’ll see a correction, you also see consolidation, but I don’t think you can get innovation without that kind of messy competition.”

Gensler’s prophetic words about the AI bubble bursting before we even reach the next presidential election are alarming, yes.

But I can envision a world where potential candidates use that moment as one of their political talking points in a bid to ease back on all that AI spending, put more into the American populace and hold back on bailing out any of the AI companies that are hurting from such a traumatic event.

All signs point to something devastating happening to the AI industry at some point, especially with the huge backlash against data centers and the politicians who are looking to put in moratoriums on their continued development.

Napoli midfielder Andre Frank Zambo Anguissa will not sign a new contract, but the club wants €6m to sell in January, with Olympique Marseille and Besiktas interested.

Privacy on Solana is ‘non-negotiable’ for institutional adoption, Mert Mumtaz says

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The Helius CEO says financial firms need confidentiality onchain and argues Zcash’s rise could push bitcoin to improve.

Privacy features are a must for bringing institutional finance onto Solana, according to Mert Mumtaz, co-founder and CEO of Helius.

"Institutions will not come onchain unless they have privacy and compliance guarantees," Mumtaz told The Block on Monday at Solana Breakpoint in London.

Mumtaz, whose company builds Solana infrastructure, said confidential lending was a big example. Financial institutions need to be able to transact without exposing private information, and moving onchain shouldn't mean accepting an inferior product, he added.

He compared it to the internet's shift from HTTP to HTTPS, which added stronger protections for sensitive information to help scale online commerce. Blockchain privacy should follow a similar path, he argued.

"Crypto without privacy is not crypto," Mumtaz said. This also extends to Zcash, which Mumtaz sees as a store of value that complements bitcoin.

"If you derive a store of value from first principles, Zcash looks very close to the ideal solution," he said.

Despite acknowledging bitcoin's longer history and wider distribution, he sees competition between the two benefiting users.

"I think it’s good for Bitcoin that Zcash is now getting traction," he said, adding that it could put pressure on bitcoin to improve.

Mumtaz was also optimistic about crypto infrastructure security amid a wave of hacks and exploits, including the worst in history — a $6.4 billion theft from crypto exchange Binance last month. He said these attacks expose weaknesses that developers can repair.

"Within a year, I think crypto will actually be much more secure than the alternative," he said. "I recognize it’ll be painful a little bit, but fundamentally speaking, this is what we’re prepared for."

With only a few days to go before GTA 6 arrives, Sony has dropped the price of the DualSense Edge by $30

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Don't say that Sony can't time a discount well - the brand has finally given its DualSense Edge pro controller an early Black Friday deal, the week of GTA 6's release. I've seen previous Black Fridays, Prime Days, and other huge AAA releases happen, and the price of the DualSense Edge hasn't budged, but as soon as it's being considered one of the best controllers for GTA 6, the discounts finally arrive.

If you're a Prime member and you order this today, there's a good chance it'll arrive with you before the 19th, when the game becomes available to play. It's a whole $30 off the cost of the gamepad, which brings the $200 price tag to a much more palatable $169 at Amazon.

The discount stands for both the Black and White models of the DualSense Edge, and the price is the same at Walmart if you want to try your luck at collecting it at a nearby store before the 19th. In the UK, the price has come down to £149.99.

The last time we saw the DualSense Edge drop to this price was back in June, but its lowest price ever is $159.99, only $10 more affordable than it is now. According to the Amazon price history data I'm seeing, it's dropped as low as it is currently only three or four times since its arrival on the shelves in 2023. Before May last year, it had only dipped its toe in the discount aisle down to $200 or even $195 - which were sorry excuses for discounts.

Personally, seeing as Rockstar won't launch GTA 6 on PC at the same time as consoles, the DualSense Edge is the controller I'd recommend experiencing the game with if you have the budget. I said a similar thing last week when I recommended other gamepads for GTA 6, but seeing as Rockstar has been in cahoots with PlayStation for launch, there are all sorts of DualSense perks you can enjoy in the game. The DualSense Edge will get access to all of those PlayStation-bespoke features, but give you more comfort and functionality than the stock controller (which is also discounted at the moment, FYI).

What are you actually getting? Besides the hold-all charging case the Edge comes in, you also get some swappable thumbsticks, two magnetic back paddles (that come in various shapes), and refined, rounded corners to the bottoms of the grips. These make a genuine difference to how comfortable the DualSense is in your hands, and trigger stop switches give you the chance to find the squeeze that feels best for you.

There's more you can do with the DualSense on the software side, and clever profile-switching buttons rest below the thumbsticks. In a practical sense, I think most players will set these up and not touch them often, so the paragraph above lists the stuff you'll actually care about when playing GTA.

What you're not getting here is any sort of enhanced battery life - so don't expect that to be any less dire than the regular DualSense. Similarly, the Edge still uses potentiometer thumbstick modules, which means they're just as prone to stick drift from prolonged use. That's a real shame, but at least you'll have access to haptic feedback and adaptive trigger resistance, which the other best PS5 candidates don't have due to licensing issues.

For more, take a look at the best PS5 controllers, the best Xbox Series X controllers, and the best PC controllers.

Saylor’s Strategy Keeps Buying Bitcoin, Adding 'A Little Orange' Through the Dip

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Saylor’s Strategy Keeps Buying Bitcoin, Adding 'A Little Orange' Through the Dip

Strategy kept buying Bitcoin through a brutal market stretch, adding 485 BTC worth roughly $33.4 million and repurchasing $228 million of its preferred stock, as chairman Michael Saylor urged the market to rally with a characteristically cheeky nudge.

The company disclosed the purchase in a filing Monday. The modest addition, small by the standards of a firm that once bought tens of thousands of coins at a time, lifted Strategy’s total holdings to 850,849 BTC as of Nov. 15, alongside $4.9 billion in U.S. dollar assets.

Strategy, the largest corporate holder of Bitcoin, remains far ahead of any other treasury company.

The buy caps a stretch of smaller, steadier accumulation paired with stock buybacks. Strategy recently added $82 million in Bitcoin while repurchasing $188 million of its STRC preferred shares, part of a shift toward what it calls a capital-management approach, buying back stock when it views the shares as undervalued rather than relentlessly stacking coins.

Bitcoin has slid sharply in recent days, trading well below the six-figure highs it touched earlier in the cycle, and the downturn has squeezed Bitcoin treasury firms whose valuations rise and fall with the asset.

Saylor, undeterred, posted the holdings update to X alongside the firm’s trademark Bitcoin tracker, and followed it with a quip aimed at the slumping market: “This dip could use a little orange,” a nod to Bitcoin’s signature color.

The purchase lands during a precarious moment for the treasury-company model Strategy pioneered. Index provider MSCI has finalized rules that could eject Bitcoin treasury firms like Strategy from its widely tracked equity indexes, classifying companies whose balance sheets are dominated by a single asset as “non-operating” and therefore ineligible.

Removal would force index-tracking funds to sell the affected shares, potentially pressuring prices and shrinking the premium these firms have long commanded over the value of their Bitcoin.

For now, Strategy is doing what it has always done, buying Bitcoin and talking it up, even as the market tests its conviction.

The combination of a small purchase and a sizable buyback underscores the balancing act: keep accumulating, defend the stock, and weather a downturn that has turned the once-unstoppable treasury trade into a far more fraught proposition.

Kava moves to shut down lending platform after hackers drain nearly $4 million

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

HARD Lend plans to permanently disable new borrowing after attackers stole 53.9 million KAVA, leaving questions over how depositors will recover their funds.

Kava plans to retire HARD Lend's borrowing functionality after hackers stole 53.9 million KAVA, leaving the lending protocol facing a substantial liquidity shortfall.

The blockchain network confirmed on Nov. 16 that the Nov. 14 attack exploited a vulnerability in HARD Lend's accounting system, allowing attackers to withdraw existing KAVA tokens through manipulated collateral and liquidation transactions.

The stolen tokens were worth $3.7 million before the subsequent market selloff, according to blockchain analytics firm Bitquery.

Kava said it had patched the vulnerability and introduced safeguards to prevent attackers from repeating the exploit. It also accelerated plans to retire the lending service, with a governance proposal expected to permanently disable new lending and borrowing.

The proposal must pass a seven-day governance voting process, while existing protective measures remain in force.

“The planned retirement of HARD Lend's legacy lending functionality is being accelerated,” Kava said, adding that it would address remaining positions through an orderly process.

The company emphasized that the breach was confined to HARD Lend and did not compromise the underlying Kava blockchain or create additional tokens.

However, the incident has raised questions about how the protocol will settle outstanding obligations after losing almost all the tokens available in its lending pool.

Kava said attackers manipulated the value of BUSD collateral within HARD Lend, allowing them to repeatedly borrow KAVA and trigger liquidations that cleared their debts without recovering the borrowed tokens.

An investigation by blockchain analysis firm Bitquery found that the attackers borrowed about 55.2 million KAVA across 127 transactions and retained 53.9 million tokens after repayments involving other borrowers.

The attackers subsequently moved about 49.2 million KAVA to BNB Chain through LayerZero.

Bitquery traced the sale of 48.4 million KAVA, which generated approximately $2.09 million in BNB and USDT.

The investigation also identified a failed transaction involving 612,416 USDT through the Relay bridge. The service rejected the transfer with a “BLOCKED_WALLET” message, preventing the requested payout to Ethereum.

November 14 evidence describes three distinct access paths: lender claims, a failed bridge order and exchange transfers.

Lender withdrawals require usable KAVA in the pool. Blocking a bridge payout or reopening exchange transfers does not itself supply that cash.

Pool and bridge observations are dated November 14. Relay record was read at 16:20 UTC; BNB Chain/Ethereum data end at 16:08 UTC. The withdrawable flag was untested. Coinone notice is not a live status feed.

Kava separately confirmed that part of the attackers' funds had been frozen and said it was working with exchanges, ecosystem partners, and law enforcement to trace and recover the remaining assets.

However, it did not disclose how much had been secured or whether the frozen funds included the USDT identified by Bitquery.

The recovery efforts have yet to resolve the more immediate problem facing HARD Lend's depositors.

Bitquery's Nov. 14 reconstruction showed that the lending pool owed users approximately 55.2 million KAVA while holding just 0.19 KAVA available for withdrawals.

The blockchain network said “Individual user deposits were largely unaffected,” but did not explain how that assessment relates to outstanding lender claims or depleted pool liquidity. It also promised further guidance for affected users as the retirement proposal progresses.

Vikings won't be leaving Minnesota for altitude acclimation

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

With quarterback Kirk Cousins back and the Minnesota defense playing at a championship level, the sky is the limit for the Vikings.

On Sunday, the extent to which they’ll be literally in the sky could limit the ability to get an eighth win.

The Week 11 prime-time showdown between the 7-2 Vikings and 8-1 49ers will happen in Mexico City, where the altitude is 7,280 feet above sea level. The Vikings won’t be relocating this week to acclimate to the thinner air.

The 49ers, in contrast, have gone from Dallas to Colorado, where they’ll train in the far lower distance of 5,280 feet above sea level.

The Vikings will have a plan to get their players ready to perform on an elevated field. Details will emerge this week.

Whatever the plan is, it won’t entail the same kind of direct acclimation in which the 49ers are engaging.

It sets up another apples-to-apples comparison regarding the plans for international games. In Week 1, the 49ers went to Australia early. The Rams went late. And the 49ers dominated L.A., winning 27-7.

This time around, the stakes are extremely high. The winner takes over the de facto driver’s seat for the top seed in the NFC.

A loss means more to the Vikings than the 49ers, as it relates to their respective divisions. San Francisco has a solid cushion over the Seahawks and Rams in the NFC West. The Vikings are caught in a three-way tie for first place in the NFC North.

Thomas Frank’s biggest Tottenham mistakes as Crystal Palace announce new manager

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Your weekly briefing on Tottenham, featuring team news and expert analysis from Sam Tabuteau

I would like to be emailed about offers, event and updates from Evening Standard. Read our privacy notice.

Thomas Frank succeeds Pierre Sage as Crystal Palace manager, signing a two-and-a-half-year deal with the south-east London club.

The Dane will be in the dugout for Palace’s trip to Coventry on Saturday, returning to management for the first time since leaving Tottenham in February.

Frank was on the Eagles’ shortlist to take over from Oliver Glasner this summer before they opted for Sage, and the former Spurs and Brentford boss is said to have been keen on a return to the Premier League.

After a difficult spell in north London, with the club sitting narrowly above the relegation zone when he left, Frank took time away from management to learn from leaders inside and outside football, as well as spending time with friends and family.

His disastrous stint at the Tottenham Hotspur Stadium has inevitably left some scars, but Frank is still widely revered for his work in the west of the capital, where he got the Bees promoted to the Premier League, stabilising them as a top-flight club on one of the division’s lowest budgets.

Frank will undoubtedly have learnt some lessons from his seven-month spell at Spurs.

Here, Sam Tabuteau identifies the three biggest mishaps of Frank’s Tottenham tenure…

Part of what made Frank such an engaging character at Brentford was his ability to talk openly and honestly about the club’s culture, tactics and wider societal issues while maintaining an endearingly British sense of humour.

At times self-deprecating and sarcastic, Frank earned plenty of admirers for the way in which he humanised what can often be a cut-throat and emotionally vacant industry.

After nine years at Brentford, nearly seven as head coach, Frank had earned the luxury of being able to talk so candidly. He was comfortable in his surroundings, and safe in the knowledge that Brentford had not sacked a manager since 2015.

However, at Tottenham, he was starting from scratch at a club whose ambitions don’t leave much room for home truths.

Coming off the back of their Europa League triumph, Spurs supporters wanted to hear how the club was going to push forward.

Yet in his first press conference as Spurs manager, Frank joked that if there was one thing he could promise, it was that they would lose games.

Frank appointed new Crystal Palace manager

Frank’s first words as Palace manager as former Spurs boss appointed

Arteta facing new Arsenal dilemma after inevitable Gyokeres goals

Discover a hidden island paradise in the Indian Ocean

His ill-timed quip did not go down well, regardless of the spirit in which it was intended, and from that point on Frank was fighting a losing battle to convince supporters he understood the culture of the club.

Losing supporters after tame Chelsea defeat

Given how it ended, Frank’s tenure started surprisingly positively, with Tottenham third in the Premier League table after nine games.

The mood quickly changed, though, after a dispiriting 1-0 home defeat to London rivals Chelsea, in which Spurs managed just one shot on target.

Frank’s pragmatic style worked well at Brentford because they had one of the most efficient frontlines in the division and were not expected to dominate possession.

At Spurs, however, Frank’s risk-averse style combined with an attacking unit missing Dejan Kulusevski and James Maddison meant they relied heavily on set-piece goals and struggled to create chances in open play.

Frank’s inability to evolve his style, coupled with Spurs’ shortcomings in attack, culminated in a performance against Chelsea that left many supporters disillusioned and would come to symbolise his time in charge.

Another example of Frank’s naivety came during January’s stoppage-time defeat to Bournemouth, when the 53-year-old was pictured pre-match walking around the pitch at the Vitality Stadium with an Arsenal-branded coffee cup.

Seemingly none the wiser, Frank stood with the cup in hand while some staff members could not hide their shock, and the resulting pictures went viral as Spurs slumped to an excruciating defeat.

After the game, Frank’s blunder took centre stage, and his mystified reaction just about summed up the growing rift between him and Spurs’ supporters.

While Spurs fans vented their anger and embarrassment online and in the stands, Frank could not begin to understand how an innocent mistake could be blown so far out of proportion.

From that point on, it was clear that the narrative was quickly beginning to slip from Frank’s grasp and Spurs would lose their next five league games before he was relieved of his duties.

Thomas Frank’s first words as Crystal Palace manager revealed as former Tottenham boss appointed

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Thomas Frank has been appointed as Crystal Palace’s new manager after Pierre Sage was sacked last week.

The Frenchman lost his job on Thursday after a disastrous, short-lived spell that dated back as soon as last summer, when he was selected to succeed Oliver Glasner at Selhurst Park.

The Eagles sit bottom of the Premier League table as a result of seven defeats in 10 games, with just two victories.

The south Londoners opted to make a change after their 3-1 home defeat by Liverpool, and they have plumped for the former Brentford and Tottenham head coach as their new boss.

The Dane has signed a two-and-a-half year deal in south London, and he hailed his new side as “iconic” in his first words in his new position.

"It's an honour to sit here and be in charge of this fantastic football club,” Frank told Palace’s club media upon his appointment announcement.

“I'm really, really looking forward to it.

"Crystal Palace is an iconic club in England and in the Premier League. I will do everything I possibly can to help and support the players and everyone around the place, and I'm really looking forward to meeting them.”

Frank’s first game in charge of his new side will come against Coventry City on Saturday, as the Eagles travel to Warwickshire looking for just their third victory of the campaign.

A home Europa League encounter with Real Sociedad will follow as the new head coach takes his bow in front of the Selhurst support, and a first home league game follows next weekend, when newly-promoted Hull City travel to the capital.

Calciomercato Napoli, le cifre dell’ultima sessione estiva: non c’è solo la plusvalenza di Gutierrez

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

E’ stato reso noto il bilancio annuale del Napoli. Il documento svela le cifre del mercato estivo 2026 degli azzurri. la società partenopea ha illustrato le principali movimentazioni economiche legate alle acquisizioni e alle cessioni dei diritti alle prestazioni sportive dei calciatori. Nel dettaglio, il club di Aurelio De Laurentiis ha comunicato di aver realizzato plusvalenze complessive pari a 25,9 milioni di euro attraverso operazioni di cessione a titolo definitivo concluse dopo il 30 giugno 2026. A queste si aggiungono ulteriori 1,5 milioni di euro derivanti dalle cessioni a titolo temporaneo, confermando il contributo delle operazioni in uscita anche nella prima parte del nuovo esercizio contabile. Sul fronte degli acquisti, invece, il Napoli ha comunicato di aver effettuato investimenti per 10,7 milioni di euro relativamente alleacquisizioni a titolo definitivo dei diritti alle prestazioni sportive dei calciatori. Tra gli acquisti, figurano Benoit Badiashile dal Chelsea in prestito con diritto di riscatto e Costantino Favasuli dal Catanzaro in prestito con obbligo di riscatto. Dal Sion è arrivato invece a titolo definitivo Dinis Rodrigues. Particolarmente importanti i riscatti di Rasmus Højlund dal Manchester United per 44 milioni di euro, Alisson Santos dallo Sporting Lisbona per 16,5 milioni e Vanja Milinkovic-Savic dal Torino per 6,5 milioni. Sono rimasti in azzurro, dopo le rispettive esperienze in prestito, anche Noa Lang, Lorenzo Lucca, Luca Marianucci, Rafa Marín e Jens Cajuste, sulla base delle operazioni riportate nei prospetti. In uscita, il Napoli ha ceduto Miguel Gutierrez al Bayern Leverkusen. La sua cessione risulta quella economicamente più rilevante. Romelu Lukaku si è trasferito al Fenerbahce. Hanno salutato Napoli anche Juan Jesus, svincolato, Eljif Elmas, rientrato al Lipsia, e Pasquale Mazzocchi, passato al Venezia in prestito con obbligo di riscatto condizionato. Walid Cheddira si è trasferito all’Avellino, mentre Luis Hasa al Frosinone, AlessioZerbin al Frosinone e Coli Saco al Riga FC. Il Torino e l’Udinese hanno inoltre riscattato rispettivamente Giovanni Simeone e Alessandro Zanoli. Numerosi i prestiti: Michael Folorunsho e Cyril Ngonge al Monza, Jesper Lindstrøm al Salisburgo, Nosa Edward Obaretin al Lorient, Emanuele Rao al Pisa e Giuseppe Ambrosino al Modena. La società azzurra ha inoltre sostenuto costi per ulteriori 3 milioni di euro per acquisizioni a titolo temporaneo. Complessivamente, dunque, gli investimenti comunicati dal club partenopeo per le operazioni in entrata successive alla chiusura dell’esercizio ammontano a 13,7 milioni di euro. Le informazioni contenute nel documento contabile non riportano, in questa specifica sezione, i nominativi dei calciatori coinvolti nelle singole trattative, limitandosi a fornire un quadro generale delle operazioni perfezionate dopo il 30 giugno. È importante sottolineare come i 25,9 milioni di euro di plusvalenze non corrispondano necessariamente agli incassi complessivi derivanti dalle cessioni. Le plusvalenze rappresentano infatti la differenza tra il corrispettivo riconosciuto per il trasferimento e il valore netto contabile residuo del diritto alle prestazioni sportive del calciatore. Si tratta, in ogni caso, di indicazioni significative in vista del prossimo bilancio del club partenopeo, relativo all’esercizio 2026/2027, sul quale ricadranno gli effetti economici di queste operazioni di calciomercato.

17 SKI Editor-Approved Gifts For Hardcore Skiers

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

If you buy through our links, we may earn an affiliate commission. This supports our mission to get more people active and outside.Learn about Outside Online's affiliate link policy

(Photo: Courtesy of DryGuy, Smith, Peak Performance, Hyperice, The North Face, Skida, Heather Hansman, Black Diamond)

Hardcore skiers can be difficult to shop for.

The more involved you become as a skier, the more specific your wants and needs get. Suddenly, the standard goggles no longer apply, and you choose your jacket not based on what looks cool but on what functions in the ways you need. If you have a dedicated skier in your life, it’s not always obvious what to gift them, especially since you’ll want them to use it and love it.

To help ease your mind, we’ve rounded up some of our favorite gift ideas for the people in our lives who eat, sleep, and breathe skiing: from powderhounds to corduroy snobs, this list has a little something for everyone. And we can guarantee it—we’re somewhat of ski hardos ourselves.

We all have that one friend who complains a little too much about being cold. But when your butt freezes the second you sit on the lift, can you blame them? That’s where insulated knickers come in. Throw them on over your base layers for extra protection your derrière didn’t know it needed. Wear them over your base layer for some extra warmth or on their own for a more loose-fitting base under your ski pants. These are the perfect gift for the skier you know who likes to get a lot of days in and stay nice and toasty on the hill. —Giorgia Menetre, Senior Social Media Strategist

Good goggles don’t need to cost a fortune. Dependable click-in, click-out interchangeable lenses are a staple for a reason—they’ll get you through just about every condition.  Smith’s OG reliable goggles offer a wide field of vision, ChromaPop lenses that make the view pop, and a flexible, comfortable frame. Lenses swap easily, with no worry of a magnetic slip-up. Don’t let another ski day go by letting the skier you love squint through bad light. —GM

These booties come with a high price tag, but they’ll be the gift hardcore skiers never forget. This updated compression boot from Normatec takes the pressurized, full-length boot system that has proven recovery patterns and adds heat. Dynamic air circulation throughout the boot’s treatment session helps muscles recover faster and reduces soreness. Remember how good it feels to put an achy and tired calf next to a hot tub jet? Now, imagine that level of recovery from the comfort of your couch. Boots come in three sizes and can fit a wide range of heights.

Please note that boots will ship the week of 11/23. —Jamie Aranoff, SKI Senior Editor

Hestra’s Fall Line mittens are consistently ranked as some of the warmest by our testers. The mittens have a cozy inner layer of brushed polyester and foam to keep hands insulated without overheating. The outside is leather that, once broken in, is soft and dexterous. Wrist straps ensure that the mittens stay with the skier, and not at the bottom of a chairlift. The mittens are on the pricier side, but will surely be a gift that skiers cherish for years. The same warmth also comes in a glove. —JA

Read our full review and browse other warm gloves and mittens here.

Vests are underrated heroes for skiers: they’re a great midlayer for touring and warmer days, easy to layer, and stylish enough to wear around. The Helium Utility down vest from Peak Performance checks all three of these boxes and more. The vest is incredibly lightweight and packs down into its pocket. More importantly, it’s windproof and water-repellent. A cinch at the bottom allows for customization for the right fit. —JA

If you’ve got an impossible-to-shop-for skier on your list, then look no further: this adventure camera from Nocs Provisions (yes, the same brand that makes binoculars) is sure to please. The camera is incredibly easy to use, even with thick gloves or mittens. Its simple one-button technology allows skiers to easily whip it out of a pocket and hit record (it takes both photos and videos). There’s no SD card needed, and content is easy to transfer to either a phone or computer with cords included. The camera takes retro-style photos and videos and comes with a lifetime “no matter what” policy. —JA

For any serious skier, Dermatone chapstick is the ultimate stocking stuffer. Not only will Dermatone protect lips from chapping, but the SPF keeps skin from burning. It also belongs in every first aid kit; it’s proven to help stop frostbite on extremities like lips, noses, and cheeks. Most ski resorts sell Dermatone somewhere in the shop, but here’s your chance to stock up and avoid inflated prices. Your lips, nose, cheeks, and just about everywhere else will thank you. —JA

Fleeces are always a solid gift, but Ridge Merino’s Alpha Wool Insulated jacket goes the extra mile. This fleece is designed to act as an outer layer for warmer days or uphilling, and a midlayer for when temperatures drop. What makes this fleece different is a mix of strategically placed wool to wick away moisture in places you sweat most, matched with a water-resistant Pertex Quantum Air that acts more like a shell than a fleece.  The fleece is complete with a hood, so no matter the weather, you’ll be prepared.—JA

The North Face just inked a deal with U.S. Ski & Snowboard to outfit the men’s and women’s alpine and freestyle teams for the next eight years. Bonus: They just released a version of the kit for the skiing public. The Comp Team Gore-Tex 2L Freeride and 3L Alpine jackets and pants come in alpine and freestyle designs, but the tech is the same: fully wind- and waterproof with plenty of features such as helmet-compatible hoods,  pit and thigh zips, and a multitude of smartly placed pockets. Don’t forget about the U.S. Ski Team crest on the right chest and all of the relevant sponsor badges to complete the look for your U.S. Ski Team superfan. —Samantha Berman, SKI Executive Editor

Here’s the perfect stocking stuffer for the uphiller on your list—or anyone who wants to add a little flair to their kit. Skida’s Pursuit Headband features the new Alpenbloom print, designed by U.S. Ski Teamer Paula Moltzan as part of the brand’s 2026–’27 collaboration with U.S. Ski & Snowboard. Beyond the athlete-designed pattern, the headband is a genuinely useful piece, made with a moisture-wicking, quick-drying fabric that regulates temperature without trapping too much heat and cozy fleece lining for comfort. A secure, nonslip fit keeps it in place whether you’re skiing, skinning, or working up a sweat on a cold-weather run. For guys, check out Ryan Cochran-Siegle’s Under the Lights print, which comes in a hat and a neckwarmer in addition to a headband.—SB

The Black Diamond Spark Mitts have been a tester favorite for years, and for good reason: you can’t go wrong with a short gauntlet mitt with a full leather outer. But the Spark goes beyond boring old performance. Every few seasons, Black Diamond drops a colorway with a fun, embroidered design on the inner side of the index finger. The Angel Collinson mustache model was our longtime favorite, but the current model with shark teeth is just as fun—and a good reminder that skiing isn’t so serious. —Jenny Wiegand, Outside Associate Gear Director

For the skier who skis in all conditions, this is the midlayer to give. At just 9.8 ounces, the Lightstream Full Zip stays unbulky while delivering serious warmth from -10 to 50 degrees. Vivo Extreme insulation is zoned for the job: 60 grams in the body and collar, 40 in the hood and sleeves, and 25 in the underarms and side seams. The 15-denier stretch nylon/PU ripstop shell is tough enough for daily abuse, and it also works as an outer layer for fall bike commutes and dog walks. Pair it with the Lightstream Insulated Short, made for skiers who run cold but won’t give up their shell pants, even on resort days. Full-length side zips let you take the shorts off when things heat up, without removing your ski pants. Light and packable, they’re also the ultimate emergency layer to stash in your boot bag. Available for men and women. —JW

Carv is the perfect gift for the skier who has everything, mostly because it’s the kind of gadget they’d struggle to justify buying for themselves. But if someone gave it to them? Game on. I’ll admit, I was skeptical of this digital ski coach, which uses sensors attached to your boots to analyze your technique and deliver real-time feedback. But after a few seasons, I’m hooked. It turns improving your skiing into a game, and it’s surprisingly addictive. Ted Ligety once told me his secret to topping the Carv leaderboard: find a stretch of groomer where you can make 10 perfect turns, then ski it over and over until you top the leaderboard. Personally, I might get distracted looking for powder, but I’ve found Carv makes even an ordinary groomer day more interesting. It’s fun, genuinely useful, and exactly the sort of gift someone might not know they wanted. Editor’s note: The list price includes a 12-month membership; continued coaching requires renewal. —Sierra Shafer, Editorial Director, Lifestyle

Few things in skiing are more universally useful than a Voilé Strap. These simple, stretchy polyurethane straps are the duct tape of the ski world, minus the sticky residue. Need to secure your skis together? Voilé Strap. Something breaks on your pack? Voilé Strap. Need to attach something to something else in a way the manufacturer probably didn’t intend? You get the idea. Made in Utah, these little problem-solvers come in a variety of lengths and colors and are practically indestructible. Every skier should have a couple tucked into their pack. Or, if you’re like me, scattered around the garage, the gear room, and the back of the car. You never know when you’ll need one, but trust me, you will. —SS

Why do we organize entire lives around sliding down snowy mountains? It’s a fair question, especially when you consider the expense, questionable career decisions, and occasional sleeping arrangements involved in chasing winter. In Powder Days, journalist Heather Hansman sets out to understand the people who’ve made skiing more than a hobby, exploring the history of ski-bum culture and what happens when the mountain towns that made it possible become increasingly difficult places to live. It’s funny, thoughtful, and at times uncomfortable in the best way, asking big questions about access, affordability, and the future of a sport we love. Give it to the skier who plans their life around winter, or anyone who’s ever seriously considered quitting their job to ski more. —SS

I didn’t win a single race in Alta’s town race series last winter, but I did win this boot dryer in the end-of-season raffle, and frankly, I count that as a victory. Wet ski boots aren’t just smelly. Moisture in your liners compromises insulation, making your feet cold even on mild days and potentially cutting a perfectly good ski day short. And if that moisture freezes overnight? Even worse. This compact dryer uses gentle heat and forced air to dry your boots without damaging their liners. What I love most is that it plugs into both a standard outlet and your car’s 12-volt adapter, so you can start drying your boots on the drive home. It’s also small enough to toss in your ski bag for a trip. Not glamorous, but genuinely one of the most useful things I own. —SS

Scrambled Eggs Get A Major Protein And Flavor Boost When You Add This Omega-Rich Ingredient

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

We may receive a commission on purchases made from links.

When many of us think "hot breakfast," we tend to naturally gravitate toward thoughts of scrambled eggs. Aside from the fact that, when prepared correctly, they are tender, delicate, and fluffy (or custardy, depending on how you like them), eggs are considered the gold standard for protein, so they are quite wholesome as well. On their own, though, they can get a little monotonous, especially if you're making them every day, so it does help to add an ingredient here or there. And if you happen to enjoy omega-3-rich cold-smoked salmon, well, it pairs seamlessly with a classic scramble, boosting both the flavor and the protein content.

Once considered an expensive delicacy, smoked salmon is quite popular now in the U.S., and as a result, it's much easier to find at your local grocery store (even Aldi carries its own version, and it can be found at Walmart, too). Cold-smoked salmon, despite the fact that it tends to be packed in super-thin slices, is buttery and velvety. While it does taste fishy, there are also light, smoky notes that give it a deeply savory flavor, which works beautifully when combined with the mild egg scramble. Additionally, a serving (which will vary in size) might contain anywhere from 12 to 18 grams of protein, which is great if you have daily goals you want to meet or you're just looking to get more of the macronutrient in general.

Truly, the most difficult part about incorporating smoked salmon into your scramble is cooking the eggs to the right consistency without burning or browning them. All you have to do with the salmon is slice the ultra-thin pieces into slivers or small squares. Once your eggs have reached the texture you desire, you'll take the pan off the heat and fold in the salmon. That's literally it; you don't have to actually cook the salmon over direct heat because it's so delicate that the residual warmth of the scrambled eggs will gently bring it up to temperature.

Now, because smoked salmon is so light and thin, you might want your eggs to match that energy, so create a fluffy, soft texture with a bit of cream whisked in. Alternatively, you could take a page out of Bobby Flay's book and fold in a dollop of crème fraîche, or really up the decadence and melt in some cubes of cream cheese (and take care not to overcook anything, because smoked salmon with burnt, rubbery eggs? No thanks). Also, because all of the elements involved are either rich or thick, a sprinkling of something green, like fresh chives or dill, or even a little parsley or tarragon, would brighten things up, both aesthetically and in terms of flavor.

John Summit Blasts Grammys After Dance Snub: “It’s All a F***ing Joke”

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

"I’m not just talking about myself either there’s zero respect for real electronic dance music & dj's," Summit wrote on X.

John Summit blasted the Grammys on Monday morning after the popular DJ didn’t earn any nominations this year, posting a tweet calling the awards “a fucking joke” and vowing he won’t submit again unless the Recording Academy institutes significant changes.

“I’m not just talking about myself either there’s zero respect for real electronic dance music & dj’s,” Summit said. “The whole dance category is just music industry fueled pop stars.”

This year’s nominees for best dance/electronic recording are Madonna, Fred Again.., Slayyyter, Tame Impala, Tinashe and underscores. Madonna, Slayyyter and Tame Impala got nods for best dance/electronic album too, joined by Skrillex and Robyn. Related Stories Music Beyoncé Becomes First Artist to Receive 100 Grammy Nominations Music IVE, BINI, KiiiKiii, SB19, LiSA and Babymetal Score Grammy Nominations in New Asian Pop Category

never submitting to the grammys again unless shit changes, it’s all a fucking joke. i’m not just talking about myself either there’s zero respect for real electronic dance music & dj’s. the whole dance category is just music industry fueled pop stars

In a follow-up post, Summit wrote that he was “not saying the artists nominated aren’t good, they’re just not dance music, they’re pop.”

“No dj is playing that music in the clubs,” he said.

To some extent, Summit’s gripes reflect a more widespread critique within the dance categories and how it’s converged with more mainstream pop. In 2023, the Recording Academy launched a new category, best pop dance recording, as a means of giving more mainstream acts a spot for recognition while opening up more of the field for traditional DJs.

Still, acts like Madonna, Slayyyter and Tinashe are hardly strangers to the dance world, all having consistently released dance music across their entire careers.

Summit isn’t the first act to air his gripes at the Grammys and decide to boycott the Academy altogether. The Weeknd, for instance, famously boycotted the awards for years after his album After Hours was snubbed. He ended his boycott during the Grammy Awards in 2025.

The moment that galvanised England to deliver major statement against Spain

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

The Three Lions shone at the Metropolitano, stunning the world champions with, as Miguel Delaney details, a key moment providing focus to their mission after World Cup heartache

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Spain vs England was a game that had almost everything, and may yet mean more; perhaps the most significant moment might have come before it had even started.

The hosts took the opportunity of a first match back in Madrid since the World Cup to showcase the trophy, in what almost felt another homecoming.

The mood was very much of revelling in their embossed status, something that would have had a particular edge for this specific nation.

No country that has won the World Cup has waited as long for another trophy as England.

And, as the 2010 winners Iker Casillas, Fernando Torres and Fernando Llorente presented Unai Simon that with that precious piece of brilliant gold, it was almost literally in England’s faces.

Thomas Tuchel’s side had to watch the goalkeeper wave the World Cup about right in front of them.

It had been noted on the Spanish side that Jude Bellingham was the only player who clapped, this now being the city that he calls home with Real Madrid. He then played like he was demanding the same appreciation, and he was not the only one.

As one figure with knowledge of the England camp said: “They will have been thinking ‘that’s what we want’.”

Seeing the World Cup so close and yet so far, with more than three years until the next edition, only deepened a mood that had been felt in the build-up to this game.

England, in the words of both Declan Rice and Thomas Tuchel, knew they were going to win.

“I could feel the true belief in the dressing room before the match that this is exactly the moment where we want to prove ourselves and push our limits,” the manager said.

They had that conviction to show what they were really about, and to show they would be the only team in the world not cowed by Spain. It was that mentality that comes when you feel ready to beat a rival that has long had it over you; that has been a benchmark.

A pace was set just moments after seeing that trophy, as a ferocious England subjected Simon to a flurry.

Just as importantly, they displayed a new level of their own, even beyond Tuchel describing it as the best performance of his time.

This wasn’t, after all, the usual counter-attacking approach to playing Spain - at least not for when England actually got so far ahead.

It was England imposing their game, an evolving ideology, on Spain.

“We are well aware which steps we took after the World Cup,” Tuchel said, conspicuously repeating this more than once.

The manager was deeply stung after all the criticism following the Argentina defeat.

And yet, much as he might bristle about it in public, it does feel as if some of that criticism has been acknowledged.

England look much more technical, but still with pace.

Throughout the World Cup, after all, there was a definite sense of his team just seeking to force their way through.

This was something much more considered and coherent.

“We wanted to get better in the deep build-up, we wanted to get better at defending in a low block and on top of it, we wanted always to impose our identity and the way we want to play on every opponent,” Tuchel remarked.

This similarly feeds into another theme of the night, which was how James Trafford’s performance may start a path that sees him become England’s No 1.

It was conspicuous that, when asked about the goalkeeper’s brilliant late double-save, Tuchel immediately pointed to his pure football effect.

“But there was a lot to do in the deep build-up as well, to overcome the first line of press, which he did very well with everyone around him,” he said. “So there was a lot to do, never lose the concentration, never lose the focus, never lose the vision, to find the free man to overcome the press and put the game into the opponent’s half.”

Tuchel clearly wants to change the very focus of this team.

And, in doing so, he wants to change its entire outlook.

There was naturally a lot of talk about “confidence” after a victory like this, which Tuchel was only too happy to immerse himself in.

“You want confidence, not only through results but confidence through performance and the result follows,” Tuchel said. “That’s the ideal process and that’s what we got.”

When Spain beat France in the World Cup semi-final with what was the performance of the tournament - “the statement performance” as Tuchel would have it - some close to Didier Deschamps’s squad felt the reason for the win had actually come much earlier.

Spain had beaten France in a series of high-profile games, above all the Euro 2024 and World Cup 2026 semi-finals. Lamine Yamal had been right to emphasise that, because he knew it played on French minds.

It gave Spain an edge. They knew they could beat France, but France were unsure if they could beat Spain.

That’s also why the frantic ending to this game in Madrid, so at odds with the first hour, may have been so important.

Had Spain equalised, or potentially won, England would have just been another team to fail to beat them. It could similarly have been the deepening of England’s own complex about Spain, since this would have been three high-profile set-backs in succession.

Even if this had just been a draw, after all, it would have felt like a defeat given it was a 3-0 lead.

It was a win, and a first defeat that Spain have endured in 44 games over two and a half years.

As such, England are not just another team the Spanish are reigning over.

They’re a team that know how to beat them, that can carry that through to a home Euros.

It might even create a bit of consideration on the Spanish side, even allowing for how assured they are in their own approach.

It might be the beginning of a proper modern rivalry.

Might, of course. England have to go and use this to do what Spain have made customary, and actually win one of those trophies.

How Are Hotels Paid When You Redeem Points, And For Elite Perks?

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

There’s a real thrill to redeeming points for stays at luxury properties, and getting outsized value. I’m sure many people have wondered about the economics of that. How much is the hotel getting reimbursed for your stay? Who is paying for any elite perks that you receive? Does the hotel care if you redeemed points or not? Let’s talk about those various points in this post…

The major global hotel groups (Hilton, Hyatt, IHG, Marriott, etc.) don’t own a vast majority of their properties. Instead, the companies have an asset light strategy, and have management or franchise contracts for the individual properties.

In some cases, large investment firms own hotels, and they may have properties belonging to all kinds of brands. In other cases, a hotel may even be family owned.

The way the major hotel groups make money is primarily through fees (they’re also increasingly directly monetizing their loyalty programs, but that’s a whole different topic). There’s typically an initial fee to open the hotel, and then on top of that, there are fees on ongoing revenue. The ongoing fees typically fall into one of two categories (and they’re typically a percentage of revenue):

Then any upside (or downside) typically falls with the hotel owner. So the major hotel groups are making money, regardless of whether a hotel is raking in the cash or hemorrhaging money.

Now, one of the reasons that consumers flock to certain hotel groups is because of their loyalty programs, which are massive marketing machines. A big part of the value proposition of these loyalty programs is the ability to redeem points.

As you’d expect, those points are redeemed centrally through the program, and then the loyalty program has to “buy” that room from the hotel. After all, the hotel owner wants to be compensated for each head in a bed, especially since they’re already paying a system fund fee, to fund these perks. That brings us to the question of how hotels are reimbursed…

As you’d expect, hotel groups don’t publicly disclose how they reimburse their properties when members redeem points. However, over the years we’ve been able to get some insights into this.

Sometimes guests are given folios that are actually intended for the loyalty program, while in other cases the reimbursement rates showed up online when they weren’t supposed to.

While the exact reimbursement policies differ by program, here’s the general concept:

So I’m totally making this up here, but if a hotel is charging $400 on a particular night, maybe the loyalty program would pay $80 for that room if occupancy is under 90%, while it might be paying $350 if occupancy is over 90%.

Note that the number of points you redeem for a stay doesn’t directly determine reimbursement for a hotel. In other words, a hotel doesn’t get paid X amount per redeemed point. Similarly, if a program offers a fifth night free on award redemptions, the hotel is getting reimbursed for all five nights, as it’s the loyalty program incurring the cost there.

Instead, points redemption rates are set by the loyalty program, and are intended to reflect the cost incurred by the program. That’s why in many cases you’ll find that redemption rates don’t correlate directly to how much a property would cost in cash, but rather also factor in average occupancy at a property.

A hotel loyalty program would probably rather you redeem your points at a $1,000 per night hotel that’s 50% full, rather than a $500 per night hotel that’s 95% full, because the latter redemption will cost the program more. Hotel loyalty programs use historical data to determine points pricing, and the costs they incur.

Let me actually give a real example from a recent stay. I recently redeemed points for a two night stay at a Wyndham Rewards property, at the cost of 30,000 points per night. The folio I was sent accidentally had the amount Wyndham had to pay, rather than the amount I had to pay.

Even though I was paying the same number of points both nights, the hotel was being reimbursed around $87 for one night, and $184 for the next night. Funny enough, while sitting in the lobby working the first morning, I overheard one front desk agent tell the other that they were fully sold out the following night, so that tracks. The first night was being reimbursed at a reduced rate, while the second night was being reimbursed based on something closer to the average daily rate.

If you have elite status with a hotel loyalty program, how does taking advantage of elite perks factor into this when redeeming points? Who pays for breakfast? Who pays for suite upgrades? Who pays for waived destination fees and parking?

The answer is that “it depends,” though here are a few general considerations:

As you can see, funding elite benefits is a combined effort between the loyalty program and hotels.

For those curious, generally individual hotels don’t know how you earned your elite status. So it doesn’t matter to them if you earned it through actual hotel stays, or just by holding onto a credit card. Presumably a front desk agent could look at your stay history, but they typically wouldn’t have any reason to do so, in this context.

This is a loaded question, and there’s obviously no single right or wrong answers. A few thoughts, though.

Hotel staff are trained by corporate to treat guests redeeming points the same way as those paying cash. Many hotel groups even train front desk staff to thank people for redeeming their points, since it reflects engagement in the program. Officially, you should expect exactly the same treatment as if you paid cash, and you absolutely shouldn’t feel sheepish about the fact that you didn’t pay cash for your stay.

Now, in practice that might not always be how things play out. There’s sometimes a back and forth between hotel owners and hotel groups. Hotel owners often want it both ways — they want the power of the major hotel group distribution channels and loyalty programs, without actually incurring the costs associated with it, whether it’s complimentary upgrades, points redemptions, or a decent breakfast.

Will you sometimes find a hotel that’s perhaps a bit condescending if you redeem points and have an issue? Absolutely, but it’s not how it should be, and it’s the exception rather than the norm. Some may remember my stay at the Aegon Mykonos, at the time a Marriott Autograph Collection property (the hotel has since… mysteriously disappeared?).

Long story short, I was downgraded despite using a confirmed upgrade, and the manager was completely unapologetic about it, and was unwilling to do anything meaningful. One of his defenses was that I was staying “complimentary,” and therefore shouldn’t be complaining.

The hotel industry is complex, given the various parties involved, from the hotel group to the individual hotel owners. Hopefully the above is a useful rundown of how reimbursement works when redeeming points. The important thing to understand is that hotels get paid a lot more when you’re redeeming points if the property is full.

When redeeming points, you definitely shouldn’t feel sheepish about it. You earned your stay, and the reason hotels belonging to major loyalty programs are able to command a revenue premium is because of the points and loyalty perks they offer.

What’s your take on how hotels are reimbursed for award stays? And do you feel like you’ve ever been treated differently when redeeming points?

Capcom Reveals Fanmade Robot Master for Mega Man: Dual Override

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Daniel is a lifelong gamer with a love for various genres and gameplay styles who enjoys exploring games and playing from various angles.

The most iconic characters in the Mega Man franchise, second only to ol’ Rock himself, are the Robot Masters, the powerful, highly-specialized robots built by Dr. Light, Dr. Wily, or whoever else who are programmed to wreak havoc upon the world, usually in batches of eight. Naturally, the newest upcoming entry in the mainline series, Mega Man: Dual Override, will have a new swath of Robot Masters to do battle with, several of which like Jet Man, Cook Man, and Twinkle Girl have already been revealed.

However, in addition to these new Capcom-made Robot Masters, Dual Override is slated to bring back a long-dormant tradition of the Mega Man series: fan-designed Robot Masters. Not long after the game’s initial reveal, Capcom launched a character design contest in which fans were encouraged to design their own original Robot Master and submit it for a chance to be included in the game.

Capcom showed off a new Mega Man: Dual Override demo at Tokyo Game Show this year, proving the veteran franchise has still got it.

Many months later, Capcom has officially announced the winner of this contest and revealed the fanmade Robot Master who will be joining the rest of the new Wily Numbers. Strap on your scouring pads and turn your spray nozzles to “ON,” because our latest menace is Cleanser Man.

As director Shinya Ikuta and producer Hiroyuki Minamitani announced in a video revealed during its TwitchCon 2026 panel and posted to the official Mega Man YouTube channel, Cleanser Man was selected out of around 10,000 entrants into the contest from around the world, which then had to be meticulously narrowed down to 20 proper participants, followed by just 6 finalists. These 6 finalists included Sweeper Woman, Recycle Man, Juggle Man, Valve Man, Cactus Man, and of course, Cleanser Man.

“In the end, we chose Cleanser Man because of the unique way the concept was brought together and the originality of the color design,” Ikuta explained.

These 6 finalists included Sweeper Woman, Recycle Man, Juggle Man, Valve Man, Cactus Man, and of course, Cleanser Man.

According to an official character summary sent by Capcom to Game Informer, Cleanser Man is a cleaning robot with an obsessive love of all things sparkling and pristine who will go to absurd lengths to scrub out any perceived imperfections, even if he ends up breaking things in the process. His stage will be set in a trash processing plant that’s flooding the world with garbage so people better appreciate his dedication to cleanliness.

The Tokyo Game Show 2026 Presentation revealed new details regarding both the second protagonist and the game's core mechanics.

Cleanser Man comes to us from a Brazilian artist who goes by “miya” on their X, formerly Twitter account. Upon the reveal that their design was chosen, they posted a screencap of Cleanser Man’s official render side-by-side with their concept art, adding the caption, “The attention to detail almost made me cry. It truly means a lot.”

Not only was the character-design contest a fun way to get people hyped up for Mega Man: Dual Override, not to mention showcase some really cool character-design work, it’s also an important revival of a long-forgotten Mega Man tradition.

Following the release of the original Mega Man game and prior to the release of Mega Man 2, Capcom held a similar contest for its Japanese players to decide the designs of that game’s eight Robot Master. This contest format would be repeated for every subsequent mainline Mega Man game up to and including Mega Man 8, resulting in many of the most notoriously-difficult Robot Masters, before being discontinued from Mega Man & Bass through Mega Man 11. While there was still some fan design encouragement, it was mostly relegated to spin-off series like Battle Network and Star Force.

While it’s only for a single Robot Master rather than a full set of eight, Cleanser Man is still the first fanmade Robot Master to make it into a mainline Mega Man title since 1996, with the added accolade that he’s the first Robot Master to come from a designer in South America. It’ll truly be an honor to shove my Buster Gun in his face.

Mega Man has had a storied career, featured in many iconic games. This list breaks down the best Mega Man games out there.

Thomas Frank appointed Crystal Palace manager after nightmare Tottenham reign

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Crystal Palace sacked Pierre Sage last week following a disappointing start to the season

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Thomas Frank has been appointed as Crystal Palace’s new manager on a deal running until the summer of 2029, with the Danish coach now tasked with helping the south London club climb up the Premier League table.

Frank makes his return to management in England following a difficult eight-month spell leading Tottenham, having previously spent seven successful years in charge at Brentford.

Palace parted company with Pierre Sage last week after managing just two victories from 10 league fixtures, leaving the south London club bottom of the Premier League during the November international break. Frank will take charge for the first time away to Coventry on Saturday.

Frank said: "It’s an honour to sit here and be in charge of this fantastic football club. I’m really, really looking forward to it.

"Crystal Palace is an iconic club in England and in the Premier League. I will do everything I possibly can to help and support the players and everyone around the place, and I’m really looking forward to meeting them."

Paddy McCarthy has been retained as assistant manager alongside goalkeeping coach Andy Quy, who was previously part of Frank's staff at Brentford, with further backroom personnel to be confirmed.

Sage originally arrived in the summer to replace Oliver Glasner following his trophy-laden tenure at Selhurst Park, but the French coach immediately faced problems.

Joining after a successful period with Lens, Sage saw key Palace defenders Maxence Lacroix and Daniel Munoz leave before the side lost three of their opening four league games.

Although Palace advanced to the Carabao Cup quarter-finals, a solitary win in the Europa League and seven defeats across 10 Premier League matches prompted chairman Steve Parish to dismiss Sage on November 12.

Frank was picked as the ideal candidate to steer Palace away from a potential relegation scrap after overseeing multiple successful survival campaigns at Brentford.

The 53-year-old took the Bees into the top flight in 2021 before helping them establish themselves in the Premier League despite having a small budget compared to rival clubs.

However, Frank’s opportunity to succeed in a different environment at Tottenham turned out to be tough despite an encouraging opening period in north London.

Amid increasing supporter frustration and a stretch of two wins in 17 league matches, Frank was sacked on February 11. He holds the worst win record of any permanent Spurs manager in Premier League history, but now seeks to succeed at Palace.

Parish said: "I’m delighted to be able to welcome Thomas to Crystal Palace. He has a proven track record of both creating winning teams and improving players, making him the perfect fit for our club.

"I’m confident Thomas’ extensive experience in the Premier League will help us achieve our goals for the rest of the season."

I'm Glad Even Chris Pratt Is Talking Guardians Of The Galaxy Now James Gunn's Schedule Is Wide Open

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

An Internet User Just Made A Comment About James Gunn’s DC Tenure That Makes Me Feel Sad And Weird

Lanterns Showrunner Responds To James Gunn's DC Departure Amid Season 2 Questions

James Gunn’s Removal And Henry Cavill Talking Superman Has Fans Frazzled, But I Have A Hot Take

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

Somebody check if Feige still has James Gunn's number.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

I thought I'd made peace with saying goodbye to James Gunn's Guardians of the Galaxy. The third movie wrapped things up beautifully, and with Gunn running DC Studios, I figured there wasn't much chance of getting the old gang back together. Well, Hollywood has a funny way of changing plans. Gunn is leaving his leadership position at DC, and Chris Pratt is already talking about the possibility of making another Guardians movie with him. Let me just state for the record that I'm right there with him.

With Avengers: Doomsday approaching on the 2026 movie schedule, Pratt's future as Star-Lord has been on my mind lately. During a recent interview with MovieZine while promoting Way of the Warrior Kid, the actor was asked whether he'd be interested in reuniting with Gunn for a fourth installment. He hadn't spoken to the filmmaker since the DC news broke, but his answer was encouraging:

He directed all three movies, and that's pretty rare… so if he wanted to come back and bring it back, I would love that.

Well, now I want it too. Gunn spent nearly a decade building one of Marvel's strangest superhero teams into something audiences genuinely cared about. Somehow, he also made me cry over a talking raccoon more times than I'd care to admit.

This conversation is considerably less cheerful for DC fans. Gunn and Peter Safran are stepping down as co-heads of DC Studios following the Warner Bros. ownership shake-up, with former Marvel executive Jeremy Latcham taking over Dec. 1, raising plenty of questions about the DC Universe's future.

Gunn isn't exactly sitting at home waiting for Marvel to call. He's still involved with Man of Tomorrow, his follow-up to 2025's Superman, which is scheduled for July 2027. He also has other DC-related work on his plate. Still, stepping away from running an entire studio should eventually give him more freedom to choose what he wants to direct. But does he want to direct a fourth GotG?

Pratt's comments are especially interesting because he previously had some pretty specific thoughts about returning as Star-Lord. Last year, I covered his concerns about finding somebody who could fill Gunn's shoes. At the time, it seemed unlikely the filmmaker would ever be available. Suddenly, that possibility doesn't sound quite so ridiculous.

There's also the matter of where we left Peter Quill. At the end of Guardians of the Galaxy Vol. 3, he returned to Earth to reconnect with his grandfather, while Rocket took charge of a new Guardians lineup. The movie's closing credits promised Star-Lord would return, though the powers that be haven't said in what capacity or which new Marvel flick he might show up in.

Pratt recently confirmed there have been conversations about bringing him back, which is encouraging. Although returning for another Avengers movie and reuniting with Gunn for a fourth Guardians adventure would be two very different things. There's still no indication that Guardians of the Galaxy Vol. 4 is actually happening. Gunn spent years saying the third movie would conclude his trilogy, and he may have no interest in revisiting those characters. I certainly wouldn't blame him for wanting to move on from comic book and superhero fare to something completely different. At this point, he's been helming superhero content for more than a decade. Maybe he wants to spread his wings and take on a different type of movie, which I would be stoked for as well.

Still, I love the idea of Pratt and Gunn finding their way back to each other, especially after everything they accomplished with the first three movies. Maybe it's wishful thinking, but if Gunn ever decides he's ready for another trip into space, I hope Marvel answers the phone.

The first three original Guardians of the Galaxy films are available to stream with a Disney+ subscription.

Ryan graduated from Missouri State University with a BA in English/Creative Writing. An expert in all things horror, Ryan enjoys covering a wide variety of topics. He's also a lifelong comic book fan and an avid watcher of Game of Thrones and House of the Dragon.

Dave Franco & Willa Fitzgerald To Lead Lionsgate Horror Film ‘Mommy’s Home’

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

EXCLUSIVE: Over the summer, Universal unleased one menacing mommy upon theaters — and now, Lionsgate is preparing to unleash another. Dave Franco and Willa Fitzgerald have signed on to star in Mommy’s Home, the studio’s new psychological horror film to be directed by David F. Sandberg.

Details as to the duo’s roles are being kept under wraps. Sandberg, the director of Annabelle: Creation and Lights Out, helms the story of a young dad whose family life becomes terrifyingly unhinged when his cryogenically preserved mom is unfrozen and sent to live with him and his family. Related Stories Casting Aaron Pierre And 'Sisu' Director Jalmari Helander Team on Action Thriller 'Fairytale In New York' From Netflix And Lionsgate Acquisitions Lionsgate Acquires Alma Har'el's 'Wet' Starring Connor Storrie, Rebecca Ferguson, Javier Bardem, Odessa A'zion, Rosalía & Lukas Haas

The project marks a reteam for Lionsgate and Franco following his starring role in the studio’s hit threequel Now You See Me: Now You Don’t, which grossed over $245 million worldwide last fall. Franco and Fitzgerald reteam after co-starring in Paramount’s 2025 Colleen Hoover adaptation Regretting You. Watch on Deadline

The script for Mommy’s Home is by James Morosini, who was attached to direct when we announced the project back in 2024. Producers include Craig Flores, Dawn Olmstead and Anonymous Content, along with Sandberg and Lotta Losten via Mångata. Nathan Kahane and Morosini will exec produce, as will Franco, joined by David Levine and Garrett Kemble on behalf of Anonymous Content.

Scott O’Brien and Maria Ascanio are overseeing the project for Lionsgate. Dan Freedman negotiated the deal on behalf of the studio, which launched international sales out of this year’s AFM.

Lionsgate Motion Picture Group President Erin Westerman told us, “Every once in a while, a script comes along that is so wildly original and impossible to forget that you just have to make it. Mommy’s Home is one of those movies. David F. Sandberg is an exceptional horror filmmaker, and we can’t imagine anyone better suited to bring this particular brand of madness to life.”

Westerman added, “Dave Franco is one of those rare actors who is endlessly watchable and impossible not to root for, even when the world around him is going completely off the rails. He’s been part of the Lionsgate family through Now You See Me, and we couldn’t be happier to be working with him again. And Willa Fitzgerald is going to be a revelation in this film. This is a project that everyone involved is making out of sheer passion, and we can’t wait for audiences to experience just how far it goes.”

Coming off the hard-R Sundance comedy Idiots and DreamWorks’ Forgotten Island, Franco will soon be seen reprising as himself in Season 2 of The Studio. This follows his Emmy nomination for a scene-stealing turn as himself in the CinemaCon-set Season 1 finale. He is repped by WME, Anonymous Content and Toczek Suddleson.

Breaking out with her turn in JT Mollner’s lauded psychological thriller Strange Darling, Fitzgerald has also been seen starring in Season 1 of the hit Amazon series Reacher, as well as Kathryn Bigelow’s Netflix film A House of Dynamite and Regretting You. Upcoming, she’ll be seen in the espionage thriller Billion Dollar Spy, as well as the Sony horror film Skeletons from Strange Darling‘s Mollner and producer J.J. Abrams. She is repped by CAA, Untitled and Jackoway Austen Tyerman.
